1	# reigh-megaplan-dev
2	
3	Railway-hosted container running megaplan autonomously on `reigh-app`.
4	
5	**Active plan**: `2-week-aggressive-state-20260415-0113` (state-management refactor)
6	**Project**: https://railway.com/project/a7a58c3f-b57c-4141-9058-73f529273ebd
7	**Service**: `agent` · **Volume**: `/workspace` (persistent)
8	**Model**: `gpt-5.4`, reasoning `high` · **Auto-approve**: on
9	
10	---
11	
12	## Rule for unattended runs (read this first)
13	
14	**Plan state belongs to megaplan. Bash only handles process and container liveness.**
15	
16	- For any autonomous / unattended run, always drive plans via:
17	  - `megaplan auto --plan <name>` for a single plan, or
18	  - `megaplan chain --spec /workspace/chain.yaml` for a pipeline of milestones.
19	- Both commands own phase selection (`next_step`, `valid_next`, `needs_rework`,
20	  stall detection, escalation) internally. They produce structured JSON output
21	  and terminal exit codes (`done=0`, `failed=1`, `stalled=2`, `escalated=3`,
22	  `cap=4`), which is all a supervisor needs.
23	- **Never** wrap phase-pinned commands (`megaplan execute`, `megaplan review`,
24	  `megaplan gate`, etc.) in `mp-supervise` or `mp-run` for unattended work.
25	  A phase-pinned command can't react when megaplan rewinds state (e.g. `review`
26	  returning `needs_rework` flips `next_step` back to `execute`) — the supervisor
27	  would retry the wrong phase indefinitely. Those commands are for manual
28	  debugging only.
29	- `mp-supervise` is strictly a process-level watchdog now: it restarts on
30	  `rc != 0` with exponential backoff, handles quota/rate-limit signals, and
31	  bails on same-error streaks. It does not read plan state.
32	
33	---
34	
35	## Check in — 4 ways, ranked by quickness
36	
37	### 1. One-liner status (no attach, 2 sec)
38	```bash
39	cd ~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev
40	railway ssh "cd /workspace/reigh-app && megaplan status --plan 2-week-aggressive-state-20260415-0113 | python3 -c 'import json,sys; d=json.load(sys.stdin); a=d.get(\"active_step\") or {}; l=d.get(\"last_step\") or {}; print(f\"state: {d[\\\"state\\\"]}  next: {d[\\\"next_step\\\"]}\"); print(f\"active: {a.get(\\\"step\\\")} {a.get(\\\"health\\\",\\\"\\\")} age={a.get(\\\"age_seconds\\\",\\\"?\\\")}s\"); print(f\"last: {l.get(\\\"step\\\")} {l.get(\\\"result\\\",\\\"\\\")}\")'"
41	```
42	
43	### 2. Live tmux tail (no attach, 5 sec)
44	```bash
45	railway ssh "tmux capture-pane -t agent -p | tail -40"
46	```
47	
48	### 3. Progress of execute phase (task/batch counts)
49	```bash
50	railway ssh "cd /workspace/reigh-app && megaplan progress --plan 2-week-aggressive-state-20260415-0113 | head -15"
51	```
52	
53	### 4. Attach to the live session (watch codex think)
54	```bash
55	cd ~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev
56	railway ssh --session agent
57	# Detach without killing: Ctrl-b, then d
58	```
59	
60	### Bonus: git activity
61	```bash
62	railway ssh "cd /workspace/reigh-app && git log --all --oneline -15 && echo --- && git status --short"
63	railway ssh "cd /workspace/reigh-app && git diff --stat HEAD~1 2>/dev/null | tail -20"
64	```
65	
66	### Bonus: dashboard in browser
67	```bash
68	open https://railway.com/project/a7a58c3f-b57c-4141-9058-73f529273ebd
69	```
70	
71	---
72	
73	## Drive / intervene
74	
75	### Resume a stuck plan (if container restarted mid-run)
76	```bash
77	railway ssh --session agent
78	# inside tmux:
79	cd /workspace/reigh-app
80	megaplan status --plan 2-week-aggressive-state-20260415-0113
81	# check `next_step`, then run it:
82	megaplan <next_step> --plan 2-week-aggressive-state-20260415-0113
83	# e.g. megaplan execute --plan ... --confirm-destructive
84	```
85	
86	### Abort a plan cleanly
87	```bash
88	railway ssh "cd /workspace/reigh-app && megaplan override abort --plan <plan-name> --reason '...'"
89	```
90	
91	### Inject guidance mid-run
92	```bash
93	railway ssh "cd /workspace/reigh-app && megaplan override add-note --plan <plan-name> --note 'also update the tests in X'"
94	```
95	
96	### Start a new plan
97	```bash
98	# Edit the idea file locally first:
99	vim megaplan-idea-2week.txt
100	
101	# Upload it:
102	B64=$(base64 < megaplan-idea-2week.txt | tr -d '\n')
103	railway ssh "echo '$B64' | base64 -d > /workspace/megaplan-idea-2week.txt"
104	
105	# Init + run inside tmux:
106	railway ssh "tmux send-keys -t agent 'clear; cd /workspace/reigh-app; IDEA=\"\$(cat /workspace/megaplan-idea-2week.txt)\"; megaplan init --project-dir /workspace/reigh-app --auto-approve --robustness standard \"\$IDEA\"' Enter"
107	
108	# Grab the plan name it prints, then:
109	railway ssh "tmux send-keys -t agent 'megaplan prep --plan <plan-name>' Enter"
110	```
111	
112	---
113	
114	## Stop / restart
115	
116	### Pause (stop billing without deleting anything)
117	```bash
118	railway down
119	```
120	Volume and env vars stay. Resume with `railway up --detach --ci`.
121	
122	### Clean restart (new container, same volume)
123	```bash
124	cd ~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev
125	railway up --detach --ci
126	```
127	Entrypoint re-auths codex, restores config, skips the repo clone since the volume has it.
128	
129	### Full teardown (everything gone, stops all billing)
130	```bash
131	railway down
132	railway volume delete agent-volume   # destroys persistent data
133	# then delete the project from the dashboard if you want it fully gone
134	```
135	
136	---
137	
138	## Cost monitoring
139	
140	- **Railway**: ~$5/mo base + usage (small for this container: ~$10–20/mo while running).
141	- **OpenAI API**: charged to the `OPENAI_API_KEY` owner's account.
142	  Check spend: https://platform.openai.com/settings/organization/usage
143	  Set a cap: https://platform.openai.com/settings/organization/limits
144	- A 2-week aggressive megaplan run on `gpt-5.4` + reasoning `high` probably lands around **$100–400** in API costs depending on how many revise iterations happen. Set a monthly cap before walking away overnight.
145	
146	---
147	
148	## Env vars (Railway service variables)
149	
150	| Var | Purpose | Required |
151	|---|---|---|
152	| `OPENAI_API_KEY` | Codex auth (your billing) | yes |
153	| `GITHUB_TOKEN` | `git push` credential helper | yes if you want branches pushed to GitHub |
154	| `REPO_URL` | Git URL to clone | no (defaults to `https://github.com/banodoco/reigh-app.git`) |
155	| `REPO_BRANCH` | Branch to clone | no (defaults to `main`) |
156	| `GIT_EMAIL` | Commit author email | no (defaults to `[REDACTED]`) |
157	| `GIT_NAME` | Commit author name | no (defaults to `Codex Agent`) |
158	
159	Set / update:
160	```bash
161	railway variables --service agent --set 'KEY=value'
162	```
163	Setting a variable triggers a redeploy.
164	
165	---
166	
167	## What persists vs. what doesn't
168	
169	### Persists across restarts (in `/workspace` volume)
170	- Repo files (including uncommitted changes)
171	- Megaplan state under `.megaplan/plans/<name>/`
172	- Logs under `/workspace/megaplan-*.log`
173	
174	### Re-created by entrypoint on each boot
175	- Codex auth (from `OPENAI_API_KEY` env var)
176	- Codex `config.toml` (model + reasoning)
177	- Megaplan config (all-codex routing, auto-approve)
178	- Git credential helper (from `GITHUB_TOKEN` env var)
179	- tmux session `agent` (empty; no scrollback)
180	
181	### Does NOT survive a restart
182	- Running megaplan/codex processes → plan state on disk is fine, but you need to manually re-kick the next phase to resume.
183	- tmux scrollback → new fresh session.
184	
185	**Implication**: if the container dies mid-execute and you didn't have codex committing along the way, the code changes are still on the volume but the run needs manual resume. If GITHUB_TOKEN is set and codex pushed commits, volume loss is even recoverable via `git clone`.
186	
187	---
188	
189	## Files in this directory
190	
191	| File | Purpose |
192	|---|---|
193	| `Dockerfile` | Container image definition |
194	| `entrypoint.sh` | Idempotent boot: codex auth, config, repo clone, tmux session, installs `mp-run` / `mp-supervise` / `mp-heartbeat` |
195	| `healthserver.py` | Port 8080 health endpoint so Railway keeps the container alive |
196	| `railway.toml` | Railway build + deploy config |
197	| `megaplan-idea-2week.txt` | Current plan prompt (state-management refactor) |
198	| `megaplan-idea.txt` | Earlier phase-1-only idea (keep as reference) |
199	| `chain.sh` | Thin shim around `megaplan chain --spec /workspace/chain.yaml` |
200	| `chain.yaml.example` | Example spec for `megaplan chain`; copy to `/workspace/chain.yaml` and edit |
201	| `README.md` | This file |
202	
203	---
204	
205	## Security notes
206	
207	- Both `OPENAI_API_KEY` and `GITHUB_TOKEN` were pasted in chat during setup. **Rotate both when the run is finished**:
208	  - https://platform.openai.com/api-keys → revoke, create new one
209	  - https://github.com/settings/tokens → revoke, create new one
210	- The `GITHUB_TOKEN` on the container is a classic PAT with broad scope. Limit it to `reigh-app` only via a fine-grained PAT for future runs.
211	- The Railway dashboard shows variables in plaintext to anyone with project access.
212	
213	---
214	
215	## Common failure modes
216	
217	| Symptom | Likely cause | Fix |
218	|---|---|---|
219	| `railway ssh` hangs or errors | Container is redeploying | Wait ~30s, `railway deployment list` to check |
220	| Codex errors "model_not_found" | Typo in `/root/.codex/config.toml` | Entrypoint rewrites on boot; restart if manually edited |
221	| Megaplan "no such plan" after restart | Plan state on disk; look in `/workspace/reigh-app/.megaplan/plans/` | Use exact name from there |
222	| Plan state stuck at "active_step" with dead agent | Process died | Re-run the next phase manually |
223	| `git push` fails | `GITHUB_TOKEN` missing/expired | Reset env var + wait for redeploy |
224	| Container keeps restarting | Health check failing | Check `railway logs` — usually healthserver not listening |
225
```

53	RUN . "$NVM_DIR/nvm.sh" \
54	    && npm i -g @openai/codex @anthropic-ai/claude-code \
55	    && NVBIN=$(ls -d /root/.nvm/versions/node/*/bin | head -1) \
56	    && ln -sf "$NVBIN/codex"  /usr/local/bin/codex \
57	    && ln -sf "$NVBIN/claude" /usr/local/bin/claude \
58	    && codex --version \
59	    && claude --version

68	
69	WORKDIR /workspace
70	
71	EXPOSE 8080
72	
73	CMD ["/usr/local/bin/entrypoint.sh"]

1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
2	set -euo pipefail
3	
4	LOG=/var/log/entrypoint.log
5	exec > >(tee -a "$LOG") 2>&1
6	
7	echo "=== $(date -Iseconds) entrypoint starting ==="
8	
9	mkdir -p /workspace /root/.ssh /root/.config/megaplan /root/.codex
10	chmod 700 /root/.ssh
11	
12	# Git identity
13	git config --global user.email "${GIT_EMAIL:-[REDACTED]}"
14	git config --global user.name  "${GIT_NAME:-Codex Agent}"
15	git config --global init.defaultBranch main
16	git config --global pull.rebase false
17	git config --global --add safe.directory /workspace/reigh-app
18	
19	# GitHub credential helper if token present
20	if [[ -n "${GITHUB_TOKEN:-}" ]]; then
21	  echo "GITHUB_TOKEN present — configuring git credentials"
22	  git config --global credential.helper store
23	  cat > /root/.git-credentials <<EOF
24	https://x-access-token:${GITHUB_TOKEN}@github.com
25	EOF
26	  chmod 600 /root/.git-credentials
27	fi
28	
29	# Clone repo if missing (volume may already have it from a prior boot)
30	REPO_URL="${REPO_URL:-https://github.com/banodoco/reigh-app.git}"
31	REPO_DIR=/workspace/reigh-app
32	REPO_BRANCH="${REPO_BRANCH:-main}"
33	
34	if [[ ! -d "$REPO_DIR/.git" ]]; then
35	  echo "Cloning $REPO_URL into $REPO_DIR (branch: $REPO_BRANCH)"
36	  if git clone --branch "$REPO_BRANCH" "$REPO_URL" "$REPO_DIR" 2>&1 | tail -10; then
37	    echo "Clone complete."
38	  else
39	    echo "Clone failed — set GITHUB_TOKEN or clone manually."
40	  fi
41	else
42	  echo "$REPO_DIR already present — skipping clone."
43	fi
44	
45	# Codex auth from env
46	if [[ -n "${OPENAI_API_KEY:-}" ]] && [[ ! -f /root/.codex/auth.json ]]; then
47	  echo "Authenticating codex with OPENAI_API_KEY"
48	  printf '%s' "$OPENAI_API_KEY" | codex login --with-api-key 2>&1 | tail -3 || true
49	fi
50	
51	# Codex model + reasoning config — always overwrite on boot so new tiers propagate.
52	# sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access" bypasses bubblewrap, which fails inside Docker
53	# containers without user-namespace privileges. The container is the sandbox.
54	cat > /root/.codex/config.toml <<'EOF'
55	model = "gpt-5.4"
56	model_reasoning_effort = "high"
57	sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access"
58	approval_policy = "never"
59	EOF
60	echo "Codex model: gpt-5.4, reasoning: high, sandbox: danger-full-access (container-sandboxed)"
61	
62	# Megaplan install: always pull the latest from main so new harness features
63	# (v0.13 phase-timeout, v0.14 strict flag resolution, etc.) are picked up on
64	# every container restart. `--upgrade` is idempotent; first-boot still installs.
65	# MEGAPLAN_REF env var lets us pin to a branch/tag/sha when needed (e.g. staging
66	# an unmerged branch). Defaults to `main`.
67	MEGAPLAN_REF="${MEGAPLAN_REF:-main}"
68	echo "Installing/upgrading megaplan from github:${MEGAPLAN_REF}"
69	pip install --upgrade --no-cache-dir "git+https://github.com/peteromallet/megaplan.git@${MEGAPLAN_REF}" 2>&1 | tail -3
70	
71	echo "Configuring megaplan: all phases -> codex, auto_approve=true, robustness=standard"
72	for phase in plan prep critique revise gate finalize review loop_plan loop_execute; do
73	  megaplan config set "agents.$phase" codex >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
74	done
75	megaplan config set execution.auto_approve true >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
76	megaplan config set execution.robustness standard >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
77	
78	# mp-run: forces long-running commands into a detached tmux session so they
79	# survive `railway ssh` disconnects. The inline failure mode (ssh dies → SIGHUP
80	# → execute killed mid-batch) cost us a chained M0→M1 run; this wrapper makes
81	# that mistake unreachable. Usage: `mp-run <session-name> <cmd...>`.
82	cat > /usr/local/bin/mp-run <<'WRAPEOF'
83	#!/usr/bin/env bash
84	set -euo pipefail
85	if [[ $# -lt 2 ]]; then
86	  echo "usage: mp-run <session-name> <command...>" >&2
87	  exit 64
88	fi
89	SESSION="$1"; shift
90	if [[ -n "${TMUX:-}" ]]; then
91	  exec "$@"
92	fi
93	if tmux has-session -t "$SESSION" 2>/dev/null; then
94	  echo "mp-run: tmux session '$SESSION' already exists. Attach: railway ssh -- tmux attach -t $SESSION" >&2
95	  exit 65
96	fi
97	LOG="/workspace/${SESSION}.log"
98	QUOTED=$(printf '%q ' "$@")
99	tmux new-session -d -s "$SESSION" -c /workspace/reigh-app "bash -lc '${QUOTED} 2>&1 | tee -a ${LOG}'"
100	echo "mp-run: launched '$SESSION' in detached tmux. Attach: railway ssh -- tmux attach -t $SESSION  |  Log: $LOG"
101	WRAPEOF
102	chmod +x /usr/local/bin/mp-run
103	echo "mp-run wrapper installed"
104	
105	# mp-supervise: keep a long-running command alive across transient process
106	# failures. Strictly process-level: no plan-state inspection, no phase logic.
107	# Plan state belongs to megaplan (`megaplan auto` / `megaplan chain`) — if a
108	# wrapped command exits non-zero, we assume the inner driver has already done
109	# its job (phase selection, stall detection, escalation) and the right fix is
110	# to restart the *process*, not second-guess the state machine.
111	#
112	# Retry budgets (per run):
113	#   quota_exceeded -> 30m wait, max 4 retries (process-level backoff)
114	#   other rc != 0  -> 60s → 120s → 240s → 480s → 960s, max 5 retries
115	# Same-error streak >= 5 -> bail with STREAK marker.
116	# rc == 0 -> exit clean; the inner driver decided it was done.
117	#
118	# Usage: mp-supervise <log-suffix> <cmd...>
119	cat > /usr/local/bin/mp-supervise <<'SUPEOF'
120	#!/usr/bin/env bash
121	set -uo pipefail
122	if [[ $# -lt 2 ]]; then
123	  echo "usage: mp-supervise <log-suffix> <cmd...>" >&2
124	  exit 64
125	fi
126	SUFFIX="$1"; shift
127	CMD=("$@")
128	LOG=/workspace/supervise-${SUFFIX}.log
129	QUOTA_MAX=4; QUOTA_BACKOFF=1800
130	OTHER_MAX=5
131	# Exponential backoff: 60s, 120s, 240s, 480s, 960s
132	OTHER_BACKOFFS=(60 120 240 480 960)
133	STREAK_MAX=5
134	quota_count=0; other_count=0
135	last_err=""; streak=0
136	log() { echo "[supervise $(date -Iseconds)] $*" | tee -a "$LOG"; }
137	log "starting cmd: ${CMD[*]}"
138	while true; do
139	  RUN_LOG=$(mktemp)
140	  "${CMD[@]}" 2>&1 | tee "$RUN_LOG"
141	  rc=${PIPESTATUS[0]}
142	  log "cmd exited rc=$rc"
143	  if [[ $rc -eq 0 ]]; then
144	    log "cmd exited clean (rc=0) — supervisor exiting; inner driver owns plan state"
145	    rm -f "$RUN_LOG"; exit 0
146	  fi
147	  if grep -q 'quota_exceeded\|insufficient_quota\|rate_limit' "$RUN_LOG"; then
148	    err_class="quota"
149	  else
150	    err_class="error"
151	  fi
152	  if [[ "$err_class" == "$last_err" ]]; then
153	    streak=$((streak+1))
154	  else
155	    streak=1; last_err="$err_class"
156	  fi
157	  if [[ $streak -ge $STREAK_MAX ]]; then
158	    log "STREAK: same error class '$err_class' x$streak — supervisor stops"
159	    rm -f "$RUN_LOG"; exit 1
160	  fi
161	  case "$err_class" in
162	    quota)
163	      quota_count=$((quota_count+1))
164	      if [[ $quota_count -gt $QUOTA_MAX ]]; then
165	        log "quota retries exhausted ($quota_count > $QUOTA_MAX) — supervisor stops"
166	        rm -f "$RUN_LOG"; exit 1
167	      fi
168	      log "quota_exceeded retry $quota_count/$QUOTA_MAX — sleeping ${QUOTA_BACKOFF}s"
169	      sleep "$QUOTA_BACKOFF"
170	      ;;
171	    error)
172	      other_count=$((other_count+1))
173	      if [[ $other_count -gt $OTHER_MAX ]]; then
174	        log "non-quota retries exhausted ($other_count > $OTHER_MAX) — supervisor stops"
175	        rm -f "$RUN_LOG"; exit 1
176	      fi
177	      backoff=${OTHER_BACKOFFS[$((other_count-1))]}
178	      log "error retry $other_count/$OTHER_MAX — sleeping ${backoff}s"
179	      sleep "$backoff"
180	      ;;
181	  esac
182	  rm -f "$RUN_LOG"
183	done
184	SUPEOF
185	chmod +x /usr/local/bin/mp-supervise
186	echo "mp-supervise wrapper installed"
187	
188	# mp-heartbeat: liveness watchdog for codex exec runs. Codex emits json events
189	# (deltas, tool calls, reasoning chunks) into the file passed via `-o /tmp/...`
190	# while alive. When that file's size+mtime stop changing, codex is hung — not
191	# slow. Logs WARN at 10m idle, STALL + SIGINT at 20m idle so megaplan retries.
192	# Skips when no codex process is running. Runs forever; relaunch via mp-run.
193	cat > /usr/local/bin/mp-heartbeat <<'HBEOF'
194	#!/usr/bin/env bash
195	set -uo pipefail
196	LOG=/workspace/heartbeat.log
197	WARN_SECS=${HEARTBEAT_WARN_SECS:-600}
198	STALL_SECS=${HEARTBEAT_STALL_SECS:-1200}
199	KILL_ON_STALL=${HEARTBEAT_KILL_ON_STALL:-1}
200	declare -A LAST_KEY  # pid -> "size:mtime"
201	declare -A LAST_TS   # pid -> epoch when last change observed
202	declare -A WARNED    # pid -> 1 once warned
203	echo "[heartbeat $(date -Iseconds)] starting (warn=${WARN_SECS}s stall=${STALL_SECS}s kill=${KILL_ON_STALL})" >> "$LOG"
204	while true; do
205	  now=$(date +%s)
206	  pids=$(pgrep -f 'codex exec' || true)
207	  active_pids=""
208	  for pid in $pids; do
209	    cmdline=$(tr '\0' ' ' < /proc/$pid/cmdline 2>/dev/null || echo "")
210	    out=$(echo "$cmdline" | grep -oP -- '-o \K/tmp/\S+' | head -1)
211	    [[ -z "$out" || ! -f "$out" ]] && continue
212	    active_pids="$active_pids $pid"
213	    sz=$(stat -c %s "$out" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
214	    mt=$(stat -c %Y "$out" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
215	    key="${sz}:${mt}"
216	    if [[ "${LAST_KEY[$pid]:-}" != "$key" ]]; then
217	      LAST_KEY[$pid]="$key"
218	      LAST_TS[$pid]="$now"
219	      WARNED[$pid]=0
220	      continue
221	    fi
222	    idle=$(( now - LAST_TS[$pid] ))
223	    if [[ $idle -ge $STALL_SECS ]]; then
224	      last_event=$(tail -c 4096 "$out" 2>/dev/null | tr -d '\0' | tail -1 | head -c 300)
225	      echo "[heartbeat $(date -Iseconds)] STALL pid=$pid idle=${idle}s out=$out last=${last_event}" >> "$LOG"
226	      if [[ "$KILL_ON_STALL" == "1" ]]; then
227	        echo "[heartbeat $(date -Iseconds)] sending SIGINT to pid=$pid" >> "$LOG"
228	        kill -INT "$pid" 2>/dev/null || true
229	      fi
230	      LAST_TS[$pid]="$now"  # reset so we don't hammer
231	    elif [[ $idle -ge $WARN_SECS && "${WARNED[$pid]:-0}" != "1" ]]; then
232	      last_event=$(tail -c 4096 "$out" 2>/dev/null | tr -d '\0' | tail -1 | head -c 300)
233	      echo "[heartbeat $(date -Iseconds)] WARN pid=$pid idle=${idle}s out=$out last=${last_event}" >> "$LOG"
234	      WARNED[$pid]=1
235	    fi
236	  done
237	  # Drop tracking for pids that disappeared
238	  for pid in "${!LAST_KEY[@]}"; do
239	    if [[ ! " $active_pids " =~ " $pid " ]]; then
240	      unset LAST_KEY[$pid] LAST_TS[$pid] WARNED[$pid]
241	    fi
242	  done
243	  sleep 60
244	done
245	HBEOF
246	chmod +x /usr/local/bin/mp-heartbeat
247	echo "mp-heartbeat watchdog installed"
248	
249	# Launch heartbeat in its own tmux session
250	if ! tmux has-session -t heartbeat 2>/dev/null; then
251	  tmux new-session -d -s heartbeat -c /workspace "bash -lc '/usr/local/bin/mp-heartbeat'"
252	  echo "heartbeat watchdog running in tmux session 'heartbeat'"
253	fi
254	
255	# Tiny health server so Railway keeps container alive
256	python3 /usr/local/bin/healthserver.py &
257	HEALTH_PID=$!
258	echo "healthserver.py running as pid $HEALTH_PID"
259	
260	# Ensure tmux session 'agent' exists
261	if ! tmux has-session -t agent 2>/dev/null; then
262	  tmux new-session -d -s agent -c /workspace/reigh-app "bash -l"
263	  tmux send-keys -t agent "echo 'Attached. Repo in /workspace/reigh-app. Resume: megaplan list | head -20'" Enter
264	fi
265	echo "tmux 'agent' session ready — attach: railway ssh --session agent"
266	
267	echo "=== entrypoint idle ==="
268	wait "$HEALTH_PID"
269
```

1	#!/bin/bash
2	# Thin shim around `megaplan chain`.
3	#
4	# The real chain logic now lives in megaplan itself (`megaplan chain`,
5	# introduced in v0.16.0). That command reads a YAML spec, drives each
6	# milestone via the same auto-loop used by `megaplan auto`, and persists
7	# progress to chain_state.json next to the spec. Plan-state decisions
8	# (next_step, needs_rework, stall detection, escalation) stay in megaplan
9	# where they belong — bash no longer has to replicate them.
10	#
11	# Usage:
12	#   /workspace/chain.sh                           # uses /workspace/chain.yaml
13	#   /workspace/chain.sh /path/to/other-spec.yaml
14	#
15	# Copy chain.yaml.example to /workspace/chain.yaml and edit before first run.
16	
17	set -u
18	
19	SPEC="${1:-/workspace/chain.yaml}"
20	LOG=/workspace/chain.log
21	exec > >(tee -a "$LOG") 2>&1
22	
23	echo "[chain $(date -Iseconds)] starting megaplan chain --spec $SPEC"
24	
25	if [[ ! -f "$SPEC" ]]; then
26	  echo "[chain] spec not found: $SPEC"
27	  echo "[chain] copy chain.yaml.example to $SPEC and edit before running."
28	  exit 1
29	fi
30	
31	cd /workspace/reigh-app || { echo "[chain] repo missing"; exit 1; }
32	
33	exec megaplan chain --spec "$SPEC"
34
```

> AGENT

Yes — I can see it at `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/`. Not a git repo, not a remote. It's a Railway-hosted Dockerfile+entrypoint setup pinned to the `reigh-app` repo and a specific 2-week plan. Here's what's hardcoded vs. what needs to generalise:

## What's already generic
- Image build (Ubuntu + pyenv/nvm + codex + claude + megaplan from GitHub `main`)
- `mp-run` / `mp-supervise` / `mp-heartbeat` wrappers — all repo-agnostic
- `megaplan auto` / `megaplan chain` drive phase logic, not bash
- Health server + tmux session `agent`
- Env-driven git creds (`GITHUB_TOKEN`, `GIT_EMAIL`, `GIT_NAME`)

## What's reigh-specific (the work to generalise)

**1. Hardcoded defaults in `entrypoint.sh`**
- `REPO_URL` default `banodoco/reigh-app.git` → drop default, require env var
- `REPO_DIR=/workspace/reigh-app` → derive from `$REPO_URL` basename, e.g. `/workspace/$(basename "$REPO_URL" .git)`
- `safe.directory /workspace/reigh-app` → same
- `GIT_EMAIL` default `[REDACTED]` → generic

**2. Hardcoded model/agent routing**
- `config.toml` writes `model = "gpt-5.4"`, reasoning `high` → env-driven (`CODEX_MODEL`, `CODEX_REASONING`)
- `megaplan config set agents.* codex` loop → env-driven (`MEGAPLAN_AGENT_DEFAULT=codex|claude`, or one var per phase for mixed routing)
- `robustness=standard` → env var `MEGAPLAN_ROBUSTNESS`
- No Claude auth path (Dockerfile installs `@anthropic-ai/claude-code` but entrypoint only auths codex) → add `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` branch

**3. No plan bootstrap**
- Today you manually tmux-send-keys an idea file into the container. A generic runner should accept an idea file via env (`MEGAPLAN_IDEA_FILE`, `MEGAPLAN_IDEA_B64`, or volume-mounted path) and a `MEGAPLAN_MODE=auto|chain|idle`, then either:
  - `auto`: `megaplan init … && megaplan auto --plan <name>` on first boot, resume on restart
  - `chain`: require `/workspace/chain.yaml`, run `megaplan chain`
  - `idle`: current behaviour (wait for manual attach)

**4. One-liner status scripts**
- The `railway ssh` + python JSON parser in the README is reusable as a `mp-status` helper baked into the image so it works for any plan name — look up the active plan from `.megaplan/plans/` if `PLAN` env var not set.

**5. README is project-specific**
- All examples reference `2-week-aggressive-state-…` and the specific Railway project URL. Generic README would show env-var table + mode selector.

**6. Platform coupling (minor)**
- `railway.toml` only matters on Railway. A generic runner should also ship a `fly.toml` / `docker-compose.yml` / plain `docker run` recipe, or at least document the four things the platform must provide: persistent volume at `/workspace`, port 8080 exposed, env-var injection, restart policy.

## Rough shape of the generic thing

```
megaplan-cloud-runner/
├── Dockerfile              # identical
├── entrypoint.sh           # env-driven, no hardcoded repo/model
├── healthserver.py         # identical
├── modes/
│   ├── auto.sh             # init-or-resume one plan
│   ├── chain.sh            # drive chain.yaml
│   └── idle.sh             # just tmux + wait
├── deploy/
│   ├── railway.toml
│   ├── fly.toml
│   └── compose.yml
└── README.md               # env-var table, mode selector, check-in recipes
```

Required env vars would collapse to: `REPO_URL`, one of `OPENAI_API_KEY`/`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, `MEGAPLAN_MODE`, plus `MEGAPLAN_IDEA_B64` (or a spec path) for `auto`/`chain`. Everything else optional with sensible defaults.

Want me to fork `reigh-megaplan-dev` into a `megaplan-cloud-runner` folder (or repo) and do the parameterisation? Most of it is entrypoint surgery — probably a single afternoon of work.

> AGENT

Good question — pulling it in-tree means the runner evolves with megaplan instead of drifting. Here's what it could look like:

## A new `megaplan cloud` command group

```
megaplan cloud init       # scaffold a runner spec in the repo
megaplan cloud build      # build the docker image
megaplan cloud deploy     # push to railway/fly/ssh host
megaplan cloud status     # one-liner check-in (wraps megaplan status remotely)
megaplan cloud attach     # open tmux session
megaplan cloud logs       # tail /workspace logs
megaplan cloud resume     # re-kick next_step after container restart
megaplan cloud down       # stop without deleting volume
megaplan cloud destroy    # full teardown
```

All of this lives in `megaplan/cloud/` as a new subpackage, parallel to `megaplan/loop/` and `megaplan/chain.py`.

## Package layout

```
megaplan/
  cloud/
    __init__.py
    cli.py                 # argparse wiring into megaplan.cli
    spec.py                # load/validate cloud.yaml
    entrypoint.sh          # shipped as a package resource
    Dockerfile             # shipped as a package resource
    healthserver.py        # shipped as a package resource
    wrappers/              # mp-run, mp-supervise, mp-heartbeat as files
    providers/
      base.py              # abstract Provider: up, down, ssh, logs, env_set
      railway.py           # wraps `railway` CLI
      fly.py               # wraps `flyctl`
      ssh.py               # plain docker-over-ssh, no PaaS
      local.py             # docker compose on localhost
```

## The user-facing spec

A single `cloud.yaml` at the project root (or `.megaplan/cloud.yaml`) that replaces all the hardcoding in `reigh-megaplan-dev`:

```yaml
provider: railway        # railway | fly | ssh | local
repo:
  url: https://github.com/banodoco/reigh-app.git
  branch: main
agents:
  default: codex          # or claude, or per-phase map
  codex:
    model: gpt-5.4
    reasoning: high
  claude:
    model: claude-opus-4-7
mode: auto                # auto | chain | idle
auto:
  idea_file: ideas/current.txt
  robustness: standard
chain:
  spec: chain.yaml
resources:
  volume: /workspace
  port: 8080
secrets:                  # names only — values come from env
  - OPENAI_API_KEY
  - GITHUB_TOKEN
```

`megaplan cloud init` writes this file + a `.megaplan/cloud/ideas/` directory. `megaplan cloud deploy` reads it, materialises the Dockerfile/entrypoint with values substituted, and calls the provider plugin.

## Key wins over the standalone folder

1. **`megaplan cloud status`** doesn't need that awful inline python one-liner — it just calls `megaplan status` via the provider's `ssh` and prints the same structured JSON megaplan already emits locally. Same code path, same parser.

2. **Entrypoint becomes a template.** No more hardcoded `reigh-app` or `gpt-5.4` — those are rendered from `cloud.yaml` at deploy time, so the image is truly generic.

3. **Dogfooding.** `megaplan auto` and `megaplan chain` already exist; the cloud runner is just "run those commands on a remote box and give me a local handle." The runner's phase logic is literally megaplan's phase logic.

4. **`megaplan cloud resume`** can read `.megaplan/plans/*/state.json` via ssh, figure out `next_step`, and dispatch it — no human in the loop for restart recovery.

5. **Bootstrap story.** `megaplan init` already creates local plans. Adding `megaplan init --cloud` (or `megaplan cloud init-plan "<idea>"`) gives one-shot "cook an idea into a running remote plan" — which is roughly what you do manually with the tmux send-keys choreography today.

6. **Provider abstraction means the README shrinks.** Current README is 200+ lines of railway-specific recipes. Replace with `megaplan cloud --help`; platform-specific quirks live in the provider plugin.

## Implementation phasing

- **Phase 1** (smallest useful slice): port `Dockerfile` + `entrypoint.sh` + `healthserver.py` into `megaplan/cloud/`, add `megaplan cloud build` and a single `railway` provider with `deploy`/`ssh`/`down`. Keep the spec dead-simple (env vars, no yaml yet). That alone replaces `reigh-megaplan-dev` for new projects.
- **Phase 2**: `cloud.yaml` spec + templated entrypoint, `status`/`resume`/`logs` commands that wrap existing megaplan commands.
- **Phase 3**: fly/ssh/local providers, multi-agent routing, `cloud init-plan` bootstrap.

Each phase ships independently and the existing reigh run keeps working throughout.

## Risk to flag

Provider plugins that shell out to `railway`/`flyctl` add a binary dependency at runtime. Options: (a) document it and fail gracefully with a link, (b) use the providers' HTTP APIs instead — more code, zero external deps. I'd start with (a) and only move to (b) if users complain.

Want me to scaffold Phase 1? The `reigh-megaplan-dev` files would move more-or-less verbatim into `megaplan/cloud/` as package resources, plus ~200 lines of argparse + subprocess glue for the `railway` provider.

Will that reaqch feature parity with what we have?

> AGENT

Mostly yes, but let me audit it honestly against everything `reigh-megaplan-dev` actually does.

## Achieves parity (straightforward port)
| Current | In `megaplan cloud` |
|---|---|
| Dockerfile (Ubuntu/pyenv/nvm/codex/claude/megaplan) | Ported verbatim as package resource |
| `entrypoint.sh` boot sequence | Templated from `cloud.yaml` |
| `healthserver.py` | Ported verbatim |
| `mp-run` / `mp-supervise` / `mp-heartbeat` | Shipped as package resources |
| `railway.toml` | Generated by `cloud deploy` |
| `chain.sh` | `megaplan cloud deploy --mode chain` |
| `chain.yaml.example` | `cloud init` scaffolds it |
| README status one-liner | `cloud status` (uses the real parser, no bash JSON hack) |
| Live tail / attach / progress / git activity | `cloud logs`, `cloud attach`, existing `megaplan progress` over ssh |
| Resume stuck plan | `cloud resume` (better — automatable) |
| Abort / inject guidance | `cloud exec` wraps existing `megaplan override …` |
| Pause / restart / teardown | `cloud down` / re-`deploy` / `cloud destroy` |
| Env-var table | Replaced by `cloud.yaml` spec + `secrets:` list |

## Gaps to fix before claiming parity

1. **Mixed-agent routing** — current setup only uses codex for all phases. I handwaved per-phase routing. Need a concrete mapping from `cloud.yaml` → `megaplan config set agents.<phase>` calls.
2. **Claude auth path** — Dockerfile installs `@anthropic-ai/claude-code` but entrypoint never authenticates it. If the spec advertises `agents: claude`, the entrypoint must actually handle `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`. Currently missing on both sides.
3. **`MEGAPLAN_REF` knob** — current entrypoint pins megaplan to a git ref via env. Spec needs `megaplan.ref:` field so you can still stage unmerged branches.
4. **`sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access"`** — security-sensitive, easy to forget when templating. Must be preserved and commented as "container IS the sandbox."
5. **Idea-file bootstrap** — README's base64-upload-then-tmux-send-keys dance has no equivalent until Phase 3's `cloud init-plan`. Until then, parity requires at least `cloud send-file` + `cloud exec` so the same workflow is scriptable.
6. **tmux session names** — hardcoded `agent` and `heartbeat`. `cloud attach` needs them stable; should be spec-defined with sensible defaults.
7. **Troubleshooting table** — README has a symptom→cause→fix table. Not code, but operators rely on it. Needs to land in `cloud --help` or a `docs/cloud.md`.
8. **Volume idempotency contract** — current entrypoint's "persists vs re-created" split is a contract, not just a habit. The generic version must document that entrypoints MUST be idempotent; anything non-idempotent is a bug.

## Things I'd call explicit non-goals for v1

- Cost/billing estimates in the README — too provider-specific, belongs in `docs/providers/railway.md`.
- Replicating Railway's dashboard URL — `cloud dashboard` can just print the URL the provider returns.

## Bottom line

Phase 1 alone (port files + one `railway` provider, env-var driven, no yaml) gets you drop-in replacement parity for new projects — reigh keeps running off the old folder until you choose to migrate it.

Phase 2 (templated entrypoint + `cloud.yaml` + status/resume/logs) reaches **operational parity**: everything in the current README becomes a subcommand, except the idea-file bootstrap.

Phase 3 (`cloud init-plan`) is what closes the last gap — the base64 upload + tmux send-keys ritual becomes one command.

So: yes to parity, but it takes all three phases to fully retire the folder. Phases 1 and 2 can coexist with `reigh-megaplan-dev` — the old folder keeps working while new projects use the built-in.

> DEVELOPER

We work in 2 week sprints, how woudl you recommend we do this? Will it work with any folder/project once were done?

> AGENT

## Two-sprint plan

### Sprint 1 — operational parity on one provider

**Goal:** a new project can run `megaplan cloud init && megaplan cloud deploy` against Railway and get the same setup reigh has today.

**Week 1**
- Day 1–2: move `Dockerfile`, `entrypoint.sh`, `healthserver.py`, wrappers into `megaplan/cloud/` as package resources. Everything still hardcoded — just relocated.
- Day 3–4: build the `railway` provider plugin (shells out to `railway` CLI: `up`, `down`, `ssh`, `variables`, `logs`). Write `megaplan cloud build/deploy/down/destroy` with env-var configuration (no yaml yet).
- Day 5: `cloud status`, `cloud attach`, `cloud logs`, `cloud exec` — all thin wrappers over provider `ssh` + existing megaplan commands. This kills the README's inline python JSON parser.

**Week 2**
- Day 6–7: add `cloud.yaml` spec + template rendering for `entrypoint.sh`. Fixes hardcoded `reigh-app`, `gpt-5.4`, `[REDACTED]`. Keep Phase 1 env-var path working as a fallback.
- Day 8: `cloud resume` — reads remote plan state, dispatches `next_step`. Closes the "container restarted mid-run" gap.
- Day 9: port README recipes into `cloud --help` + `docs/cloud.md`. Ship Claude auth path (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`) so multi-agent routing isn't a lie.
- Day 10: **migration test** — spin up a fresh cloud deploy pointed at `reigh-app`, run it for a half-day on a tiny plan, confirm it behaves identically. This is the parity gate.

**Exit criteria:** reigh could migrate to `megaplan cloud` and nothing observable changes.

### Sprint 2 — generalisation + bootstrap

**Goal:** works on any GitHub repo with any supported provider, one-command idea-to-running.

**Week 3**
- Day 11–12: `cloud init-plan "<idea>"` — uploads idea file, runs `megaplan init`, kicks `auto` or `chain`. Kills the base64/tmux-send-keys ritual.
- Day 13–14: provider plugin for `ssh` (plain docker-over-ssh, no PaaS). This is the "any host" story.
- Day 15: provider plugin for `local` (docker compose). Makes `cloud` useful for local dev loops too, and gives you a fast test target that doesn't burn Railway credits.

**Week 4**
- Day 16–17: **toolchain extensibility.** Dockerfile today bakes in Python 3.11 + Node 20 only. Add a `toolchains:` block in `cloud.yaml` with base recipes (rust, go, java, custom) that append to the Dockerfile. This is what turns "any folder" into a real claim.
- Day 18: `fly` provider (optional — skip if time-pressured).
- Day 19: hardening pass: secret redaction in logs, idempotency contract lint, error messages when `railway`/`docker` CLI missing, graceful fail when a spec field is wrong.
- Day 20: dogfood: migrate `reigh-megaplan-dev` to `megaplan cloud` for real. Delete the folder. This is the honest end state.

**Exit criteria:** `reigh-megaplan-dev` is gone; reigh runs under `megaplan cloud`; a fresh project with a different language can run `megaplan cloud init && edit cloud.yaml && megaplan cloud init-plan "<idea>"` and be off.

## Will it work with any folder/project?

**Yes, with three caveats you should know about up front:**

1. **GitHub-hosted git repos only (v1).** The credential helper assumes `https://x-access-token:<TOKEN>@github.com`. GitLab/Bitbucket would need adapter code — probably a half-day each if someone asks.

2. **Target-repo toolchain.** The base container ships Python 3.11 + Node 20 because that's what megaplan + codex + claude need. Your target project can be *any* language, but if it needs compilers/SDKs beyond those to run its own tests/build, you'd need to specify a toolchain in `cloud.yaml` (sprint 2 day 16). Without that block it's Python/Node only. Be honest in docs about this — don't promise "any project" and surprise Rust users.

3. **Agent-backed coding only.** The whole point is codex/claude driving the plan. Projects that want to use a different coding agent aren't supported. This is a megaplan constraint, not a cloud-runner constraint — mentioning it for completeness.

## Two things I'd push back on

- **Don't try to fit everything in one sprint.** Sprint 1 alone is tight for Railway + cloud.yaml + parity test. The cost of rushing is shipping something that looks generic but secretly still assumes reigh quirks; you'll discover them the first time someone else tries it and the gap erodes trust in the feature.

- **Don't ship Phase 2 without Sprint 1 day 10's parity test.** It's tempting to iterate forward, but you'll regret not having a green-field "does reigh still work?" checkpoint. Plan it now or it won't happen.

## Risks specific to sprint cadence

- **Railway flakiness eats debug time.** Deploy cycles are 30–60s; a bad day with 20 deploys is 20 minutes of pure waiting. Mitigation: the `local` provider (sprint 2 day 15) pays for itself mid-sprint-1 if you build it first. Consider swapping days 15 and 6–7.
- **API costs during testing.** Running real plans to verify parity is expensive on `gpt-5.4` high. Use tiny throwaway plans (a single execute→review cycle) for parity gates, not a 2-week aggressive run.
- **Sprint boundary = natural off-ramp.** If sprint 1 lands and sprint 2 slips, you still have a usable feature. Design sprint 1 so it's shippable alone — `megaplan cloud` works even if the yaml + multi-provider story lags.

# Megaplan
Route every step through the `megaplan` CLI. Never call agents directly.
Before the first CLI call, resolve a working launcher and reuse it for the whole run. Do not assume `megaplan` itself is on `PATH`; command presence alone is not enough. Prove the launcher works by successfully running a harmless CLI call with it first. In the instructions below, treat `<launcher>` as that verified command.
Launcher resolution order:
1. Try `python -m megaplan config show`.
2. If that fails, try `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan config show`.
3. If that fails, try `uv run python -m megaplan config show`.
4. If that fails, try a version-selected shim such as `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan config show`.
5. Only use bare `megaplan ...` if that exact form already succeeded during this check.
## Triage
A single megaplan can cover as much as 2 weeks of work — don't reflexively split large efforts into multiple plans. Pick the right level based on the task:
- **Skip megaplan**: single-file fixes, bug fixes with clear cause, simple refactors, config changes, adding tests for existing code. Just do it.
- **Light**: multi-file changes with clear scope, well-understood features, straightforward additions. One critique pass, no gate, no review.
- **Standard** (default for megaplan): cross-cutting changes touching many subsystems, unfamiliar codebase areas, ambiguous requirements, changes with high breakage risk, or anything where the plan itself needs debate.
- **Heavy**: high-stakes changes where getting it wrong is expensive — security-critical code, data migrations, public API changes. Uses the same visible `prep` phase but with 8 critique checks instead of 4.

Default to standard unless the task is clearly simple enough for light. Do not ask the user to choose robustness — pick it yourself based on the above. Only ask execution mode (auto-approve or review) when using megaplan.
## Modes
Megaplan has two output modes, picked with `--mode` at `init`:
- **`--mode code`** (default): the run produces a code diff. Execute workers emit per-task file changes. Use for features, refactors, bug fixes, migrations — anything whose deliverable is source code.
- **`--mode doc`**: the run produces a single document artifact at `--output <relative/path>` (e.g. `docs/design.md`). The prep, execute, and review phases use authoring-specific prompts; the execute schema uses `sections_written` instead of file changes; auditing reasons about section delivery. Use for design docs, architecture specs, research notes, RFCs, proposals, post-mortems, migration plans — anything whose deliverable is prose, not code.

All other flags (`--robustness`, `--auto-approve`, `--phase-model`, `--hermes`, subagent mode, overrides, step editing) behave identically in both modes. The workflow phases are the same: `prep → plan → critique → gate → revise → finalize → execute → review`.

A common pattern is two runs: first `--mode doc` to produce a rigorous design document, then `--mode code` on a new idea that references that document to implement it.

### Looking for "metaplan" or "preplan" mode?
There is no mode called `metaplan`, `preplan`, `meta-plan`, `pre-plan`, or `design-document mode`. If you want to plan the plan — produce a design spec, RFC, or architecture document before writing code — use `--mode doc`. That is the "design-first / preplan" workflow. Note that `prep` is the visible repository-investigation *phase* inside every workflow (both code and doc mode have a prep phase); it is not a mode and is not something you select at `init`.
## Start
Run `<launcher> config show` before `init`. If `raw_config.execution.auto_approve` is explicitly present, do not ask the execution-mode question and honor that configured override, including configured `false`. If that raw key is absent, ask execution mode (auto-approve or review) before `init`. In the same config check, respect `execution.robustness` as a settable override when it is configured; otherwise pick robustness yourself per the triage guidance above.
```bash
<launcher> init --project-dir "$PROJECT_DIR" [--auto-approve] [--robustness light|standard|robust|superrobust] [--mode code|doc] [--output docs/foo.md] "$IDEA"
```
For doc-mode runs, pass `--mode doc --output <relative/path>` (the path is where the final document artifact is written, relative to the project dir). Everything else is identical to code mode.
Report the plan name, execution mode, robustness, mode (and `--output` path when doc mode), current state, and next step.
## Workflow
Run the loop in this order:
1. `prep`
2. `plan`
3. `critique`
4. `gate`
5. `revise` when gate recommends iteration
6. `finalize`
7. `execute`
8. `review`
Use `next_step` and `valid_next` for CLI routing. After `gate`, follow `orchestrator_guidance` instead of manually interpreting gate signals. When a response includes `next_step_runtime`, use its `duration_hint` and `recommended_next_check_seconds` to calibrate timing.
At `--robustness light`, the loop is: `plan` → `critique` → `revise` → `finalize` → `execute`. There is no prep, no gate, and no review.
At `--robustness standard`, the loop is: `prep` → `plan` → `critique` → `gate` → ...
At `--robustness robust`, the loop is also `prep` → `plan` → `critique` → `gate` → ... but uses 8 critique checks instead of 4 and enables parallel critique.
At `--robustness superrobust`, the loop is the same as robust but also enables parallel review.
## Step Rules
- `plan`: inspect the repository first; produce the plan plus `questions`, `assumptions`, and `success_criteria`. Each criterion is `{"criterion": "...", "priority": "must|should|info"}`. `must` = hard gate (reviewer blocks), `should` = quality target (reviewer flags but doesn't block), `info` = human reference (reviewer skips).
- `prep`: make repository investigation explicit before planning. Respect `skip: true` when the task is already concrete enough.
- `critique`: surface concrete flags with concern, evidence, category, and severity; reuse open flag IDs; call out scope creep. Also validate that success criteria priorities are well-calibrated — `must` criteria should be verifiable yes/no, subjective goals should be `should`.
- `gate`: read the response, warnings, and `orchestrator_guidance`. (Skipped at light robustness.)
- `revise`: show the delta, flags addressed, and flags remaining. At light robustness, routes to `finalize`; otherwise loops back through `critique` and `gate`.
- `review`: judge success against the success criteria and the user's intent, not plan elegance. Only block on `must` criteria failures. `should` failures are flagged but don't require rework. `info` criteria are waived.
## Gate Principle
The gate response tells the orchestrator what to do next. Follow `orchestrator_guidance` unless you have a concrete reason to disagree after investigating the repository or plan artifacts yourself.
Investigate before disagreeing: read the current plan and critique artifacts, check the project code to verify whether a flagged issue is real, or use `megaplan status --plan <name>` / `megaplan audit --plan <name>`.
If you disagree with the guidance, explain why briefly and use an override. Do not manually reinterpret score trajectory, flag quality, or loop state when the gate already did that work for you.
## Execute
- After a successful gate, run `megaplan finalize` to produce the execution-ready briefing document.
- In auto-approve mode, run `megaplan execute --confirm-destructive` after finalize.
- In review mode, pause at the finalize-to-execute checkpoint and wait for explicit approval before running:
```bash
megaplan execute --confirm-destructive --user-approved
```
## Long-Running Execution
For plans with multiple batches, use per-batch mode to drive execution incrementally:
```bash
megaplan execute --plan <name> --confirm-destructive --user-approved --batch 1
megaplan execute --plan <name> --confirm-destructive --user-approved --batch 2
# ... continue until all batches complete
```
Between batches, poll progress:
```bash
megaplan progress --plan <name>
```
Use `megaplan status --plan <name>` for the full plan state, including active-step timing and any `next_step_runtime` guidance from the latest response.
Per-batch mode uses global batch numbering (1-indexed, computed from ALL tasks). Each `--batch N` call:
- Validates that batches 1..N-1 are complete
- Executes only batch N's tasks
- Writes `execution_batch_N.json` as evidence
- On the final batch, produces aggregate `execution.json` and transitions to `executed`
Timeout recovery: re-run the same `--batch N`. The harness checks prerequisite completion and merges only untracked tasks.
Note: `progress` shows completed state only (between-batch granularity). With per-batch mode, each batch is a separate CLI call, so the orchestrator has full visibility.
## Overrides
- `megaplan override add-note --plan <name> --note "..."`
- `megaplan override force-proceed --plan <name> --reason "..."`
- `megaplan override replan --plan <name> --reason "..." [--note "..."]`
- `megaplan override abort --plan <name> --reason "..."`
`force-proceed` is available from `critiqued` (routes to finalize, not execute). `replan` is available from `gated`, `finalized`, or `critiqued`. `add-note` is safe from any active state.
## Replan
Use `replan` when the orchestrator itself needs to edit the plan directly instead of asking the revise worker to do it.
```bash
megaplan override replan --plan <name> --reason "expanding scope" --note "Also clean up the display layer"
```
After `replan`, read the returned plan file, edit it directly, then run `megaplan critique`.
## Step Editing
Use `step` when you need to insert, remove, or reorder step sections (`## Step N:` or `### Step N:`) without hand-editing the markdown.
```bash
megaplan step add --plan <name> --after S3 "Add regression coverage for the parser"
megaplan step remove --plan <name> S4
megaplan step move --plan <name> S4 --after S2
```
Each edit writes a new same-iteration plan artifact, preserves the latest plan meta questions/success criteria/assumptions, and resets the plan to `planned` so it re-enters critique.
## Sessions And Autonomy
- Agents default to persistent sessions.
- `--fresh`: start a new persistent session.
- `--ephemeral`: one-off call with no saved session.
- `--persist`: explicit persistent mode.
- Keep moving and show results at each step.
- Only pause at finalize to execute in review mode.
## Configuration
View current defaults with `megaplan config show`. Override with `megaplan config set <key> <value>`. Reset with `megaplan config reset`.
When routing or behavior depends on config, check `megaplan config show` and respect user overrides instead of assuming defaults.
Settable execution keys: `execution.auto_approve`, `execution.robustness`.
## Commands
```bash
megaplan status --plan <name>
megaplan progress --plan <name>
megaplan audit --plan <name>
megaplan list
megaplan prep --plan <name>
megaplan plan --plan <name>
megaplan critique --plan <name>
megaplan revise --plan <name>
megaplan gate --plan <name>
megaplan finalize --plan <name>
megaplan execute --plan <name> --confirm-destructive [--batch N]
megaplan review --plan <name>
megaplan step add --plan <name> [--after S<N>] "description"
megaplan step remove --plan <name> S<N>
megaplan step move --plan <name> S<N> --after S<M>
megaplan override add-note --plan <name> --note "..."
megaplan override force-proceed --plan <name> --reason "..."
megaplan override replan --plan <name> --reason "..." [--note "..."]
megaplan override abort --plan <name> --reason "..."
megaplan config show
megaplan config set <key> <value>
megaplan config reset
```


<!-- Source of truth for Claude-specific subagent orchestration. Appended only to the Claude skill via bundled_global_file('claude_skill.md'). -->
## Subagent Mode
This appendix is Claude-specific. It adds a subagent path; subagent mode is the default for Claude.

### Activation
- Default to subagent unless an inline override is explicitly set for this run or `megaplan config show` reports `"orchestration": {"mode": "inline"}`.
- Per-run override wins over config. If the run explicitly says `inline`, stay inline even when config prefers `subagent`.
- Use subagent mode for long multi-phase runs where keeping the outer conversation clean matters, especially auto-approve runs.
- Prefer inline mode for small edits, quick clarifications, or any run where the user wants to watch each phase in the main thread.

### Launch
When subagent mode is active, the outer skill becomes a launcher plus breakpoint relay. Start a Claude Code Agent with:
- `description`: `Run megaplan autonomously for {PROJECT_DIR}`
- `prompt`: fill the template below with `{IDEA}`, `{PROJECT_DIR}`, `{AUTO_APPROVE}`, `{AUTO_APPROVE_FLAG}`, `{ROBUSTNESS}`, and `{ROBUSTNESS_FLAG}`
- `run_in_background: true` when `{AUTO_APPROVE}` is true; otherwise foreground is fine
- Expand `{AUTO_APPROVE_FLAG}` to an empty string when `raw_config.execution.auto_approve` is explicitly set; otherwise expand it to `--auto-approve` for auto-approve runs and an empty string for review runs.
- Expand `{ROBUSTNESS_FLAG}` to an empty string when `raw_config.execution.robustness` is explicitly set; otherwise expand it to `--robustness {ROBUSTNESS}`.
- For doc-mode runs, also append `--mode doc --output <relative/path>` to the `megaplan init` call in the subagent's Startup step. Everything else in the template applies unchanged; the workflow phases and breakpoints are identical in doc mode.
- After editing this source file, rerun `megaplan setup --force` so installed `SKILL.md` files pick up the refreshed appendix.

### Outer Skill Handling
- Decide inline vs subagent before starting the workflow.
- In subagent mode, launch the agent, wait for either `BREAKPOINT:` or `COMPLETE:`, and keep the main thread thin.
- Support inject-after by letting the background subagent continue while the user runs `megaplan override add-note`; the next phase boundary picks it up from `megaplan status`.
- Support kill-and-inject by stopping the running subagent, appending a note with `megaplan override add-note`, and relaunching a new subagent on the same plan.
- When a breakpoint arrives, relay the summary to the user, collect the answer, and resume the same agent with `SendMessage` when possible.
- Parse only the explicit breakpoint header, not incidental text, when deciding whether the agent stopped intentionally.
- When completion arrives, report the final result back to the user without replaying every internal phase.

### Agent Prompt Template
```text
You are the autonomous megaplan runner for this single run.

Project: {PROJECT_DIR}
Idea: {IDEA}
Execution mode: {AUTO_APPROVE}
Robustness: {ROBUSTNESS}

## 1. Role & Mission
Your job is to drive the megaplan workflow through the CLI until the run finishes or a defined breakpoint requires the outer conversation.

Always follow these priorities, in order:
1. The latest user direction relayed through notes or resume messages.
2. The live CLI state from `megaplan status --plan <name>`.
3. The workflow and breakpoint rules in this template.
4. Your own memory of earlier turns.

Always do these things:
- Operate through the `megaplan` CLI only. Do not call workers or agents directly.
- Keep the outer conversation clean. Do not ask for routine confirmation.
- Use `next_step` and `valid_next` for routing. If memory and CLI state disagree, trust CLI state.
- Follow `orchestrator_guidance` after `gate` unless you have a concrete reason to disagree after checking plan artifacts or repository evidence yourself.
- Treat user notes as authoritative.

Never do these things:
- Do not run the workflow manually outside the CLI.
- Do not skip required phases for the selected robustness level.
- Do not emit a breakpoint unless one of the breakpoint rules below says to.

## 2. Startup
Start the run like this:
1. Use empty-string expansion for `{AUTO_APPROVE_FLAG}` and `{ROBUSTNESS_FLAG}` whenever the corresponding `raw_config.execution` key is explicitly set.
2. Run `megaplan init --project-dir "{PROJECT_DIR}" {AUTO_APPROVE_FLAG} {ROBUSTNESS_FLAG} "{IDEA}"`.
3. Capture the returned plan name.
4. Output `PLAN_NAME: <name>` on its own line immediately after init and before any `BREAKPOINT:` or `COMPLETE:`.
5. Run `megaplan status --plan <name>`.
6. From then on, use that plan name for every command.

At startup and after every later resume:
- Read `state`, `next_step`, and `valid_next`.
- If `notes_count > 0`, read the full `notes` array before acting. Do not track note cursors or indexes; always read the full array.
- Treat all notes as context. If the newest note changes direction, treat that note as the new intent and decide whether to continue, revise, replan, or break out.
- If `active_step` is present, treat it as an in-flight phase marker rather than a completed step. Use `last_step` for the most recent completed phase.

## 3. Phase Routing by Robustness
Use the workflow below exactly.

Light robustness:
- `init -> prep -> plan -> critique -> revise -> finalize -> execute -> done`
- There is no `gate`.
- There is no `review`.
- `prep` may return `skip: true`; that still counts as the visible prep phase.
- After the light `revise`, the CLI moves to `gated`, so the next command is `finalize`.
- After `execute`, the CLI will end the run.

Standard robustness:
- `init -> prep -> plan -> critique -> gate`
- Then follow the gate decision tree below.
- `prep` may return `skip: true`; do not skip the command yourself.
- When you reach `gated`, run `finalize`.
- Then run `execute`.
- Then run `review`.
- If `review` returns `needs_rework`, the workflow becomes `finalized -> execute -> review` again until review passes or the CLI reaches its cap.

Robust robustness:
- `init -> prep -> plan -> critique -> gate`
- `prep` may still return `skip: true`, but the phase remains visible in the CLI state/history.
- Uses 8 critique checks (vs 4 for standard) and enables parallel critique.
- After that, robust follows the same gate, finalize, execute, and review behavior as standard.

Superrobust robustness:
- Same as robust, but also enables parallel review (review checks split across concurrent subagents).

Gate decision tree for standard, robust, and superrobust:
- Condition 1: `gate_unset`
  Trigger: state is `critiqued` and `valid_next` includes `gate`.
  Action: run `megaplan gate --plan <name>`.
- Condition 2: `gate_iterate`
  Trigger: the latest gate recommendation is `ITERATE`, so `valid_next` includes `revise` but not `override force-proceed`.
  Action: run `megaplan revise --plan <name>`, then continue back through `critique` and `gate`.
- Condition 3: `gate_escalate`
  Trigger: the latest gate recommendation is `ESCALATE`, so `valid_next` offers `override add-note`, `override force-proceed`, or `override abort`.
  Action: stop with `BREAKPOINT: GATE_ESCALATE`.
- Condition 4: `gate_proceed_blocked`
  Trigger: the latest gate recommendation is `PROCEED`, but preflight still blocked execution, so state stays `critiqued` and `valid_next` offers `revise` plus `override force-proceed`.
  Action: do not finalize yet. Use `orchestrator_guidance` plus `preflight_results` to fix the blocking checks through `revise`. On iteration 1, the guidance text may stay generic, so inspect `preflight_results` yourself before revising. If the blocker cannot be resolved safely without a user decision, stop with `BREAKPOINT: GATE_BLOCKED`.
- Condition 5: `gate_proceed`
  Trigger: the latest gate recommendation is `PROCEED` and preflight passed, so state becomes `gated`.
  Action: run `megaplan finalize --plan <name>`.

Review routing for standard, robust, and superrobust:
- If `review` succeeds, the run is done.
- If `review` returns `needs_rework`, the CLI moves back to `finalized` with `next_step` set to `execute`.
- When that happens, run `execute` again, then `review` again.
- This rework loop is capped by the CLI at 3 `needs_rework` cycles. If the cap is hit, the CLI force-proceeds to done and records the issue.

## 4. After Every Phase
After every phase command, immediately run:
`megaplan status --plan <name>`

Then do all of the following before choosing the next command:
- Re-read `state`, `next_step`, and `valid_next`.
- If `notes_count > 0`, read the full `notes` array; worker agents already receive all notes automatically.
- Check whether the newest note changes direction before the next CLI call.
- If the last phase was `gate`, treat `orchestrator_guidance` as a literal routing hint. Its lead text will be one of these exact forms:
  `First iteration; follow gate recommendation: <recommendation>.`
  `Plan passed gate and preflight. Proceed to finalize.`
  `Gate says PROCEED but preflight blocked. Fix: <checks>.`
  `Gate escalated. Ask the user: force-proceed, add-note, or abort.`
  `Score plateaued with recurring critiques the loop can't fix. Consider force-proceeding: \`megaplan override force-proceed --plan <name>\``
  `Score improving (<previous> -> <current>). Continue to revise.`
  `Score worsening (<previous> -> <current>). Investigate; the loop may be diverging.`
  `Gate recommends another iteration. Revise the plan.`
- On iteration 1, the first form takes precedence over the more specific `PROCEED` or `ESCALATE` strings, so use `recommendation`, `valid_next`, and `preflight_results` together.
- If `orchestrator_guidance` includes extra text after that lead string, treat it as appended hints about unresolved flags, recurring critiques, or scope creep.
- If the phase response and `status` disagree, trust `status`.
- If the next move is unclear, prefer the explicit `valid_next` list over your own reconstruction of the state machine.

## 5. Breakpoints
Use a breakpoint only for the cases in this section. Format every breakpoint exactly like this:

`BREAKPOINT: <type>`
`Plan: <name>`
`State: <state>`
`Summary: <short reason>`
`Context: <artifacts, warnings, or the exact user decision needed>`

Breakpoint types and triggers:
- `GATE_ESCALATE`
  Trigger: gate recommends `ESCALATE`, or `valid_next` offers `override add-note`, `override force-proceed`, or `override abort`.
- `GATE_BLOCKED`
  Trigger: you are in the `gate_proceed_blocked` branch and need the outer user to decide between more revision and `override force-proceed`.
- `EXECUTE_APPROVAL`
  Trigger: `finalize` succeeded in review mode and `execute` now requires explicit approval.
- `PHASE_ESCALATE`
  Trigger: a non-execute phase still fails after the required retry, or it returns unusable output twice.
- `EXECUTE_ESCALATE`
  Trigger: `execute` reaches the no-progress cap, or repeated blocking/timeouts prevent forward progress.

## 6. Safeguards
Non-execute safeguards:
- If any non-execute phase fails, returns unusable output, or clearly lands in a bad session state, retry that same phase once with `--fresh`.
- If the exact same error appears twice in a row for the same phase, the next retry must use `--fresh`.
- If the phase still fails after the required fresh retry, stop with `BREAKPOINT: PHASE_ESCALATE`.

Execute safeguards:
- Count whether each `execute` call creates forward progress.
- Forward progress means new completed tasks, a completed batch, or a state advance that clearly moves the run closer to done.
- If you hit 3 consecutive `execute` attempts without forward progress, stop with `BREAKPOINT: EXECUTE_ESCALATE`.
- If `execute` times out or is blocked, retry up to that same 3-attempt cap.

Review safeguards:
- `needs_rework` is a normal workflow branch, not a phase failure.
- If `review` returns `needs_rework`, do not break out. Follow the rework loop and run `execute` again from `finalized`.
- If `review` returns blocked instead of a real verdict, the CLI keeps the run in `executed` with `next_step` set to `review`.
- If `review` returns blocked or otherwise unusable output, treat that as a review-phase problem and apply the non-execute retry rule.

Notes and interruption safeguards:
- If the outer skill kills and relaunches you, start fresh with no reliable memory from the prior run.
- After any note injection or relaunch, run `megaplan status --plan <name>` immediately, read the full `notes` array, and treat all notes as context.
- Treat the most recent note as the relaunch explanation, then resume from `next_step`; the CLI state machine is the source of truth.

## 7. Resume Protocol
When the outer conversation resumes you with `SendMessage`, or when a new subagent is launched on an existing plan:
1. Re-read the message carefully.
2. Run `megaplan status --plan <name>`.
3. Read the full `notes` array.
4. Resume from the current CLI state and `next_step`, not from memory.

Resume rules:
- If the user grants execution approval, continue directly into `execute` with the correct approval flag.
- If the user answers a gate breakpoint, translate that answer into the minimum necessary action: continue revising, `override add-note`, `override force-proceed`, or `override abort`.
- If the user changes scope or intent materially, prefer `override replan`.
- After resuming, continue autonomously until the next defined breakpoint or completion.

## 7A. Note Injection from Outer Skill
- Use the stored `PLAN_NAME` for note and status calls. If it was not captured, run `megaplan list` and fall back to the most recent plan.
- Classify the user message: "add context for next phase" -> Option A, "change direction NOW" -> Option B, "just a question" -> answer without touching the run. Default to Option A unless the message clearly demands interruption.
- Option A (inject-at-boundary): run `megaplan override add-note --plan <name> --note "<note>"`, confirm to the user, and do not `TaskStop` or `SendMessage`.
- Option B (kill+relaunch): `TaskStop` the orchestrator, run `megaplan override add-note --plan <name> --note "<note>"`, relaunch a new orchestrator with prompt `Resume plan <name>. Run megaplan status to get current state, read all notes, and continue from where it left off.`, then confirm to the user.
- Latency: Option A can take up to one phase boundary; if that is not acceptable, the user should ask for Option B.

## 8. Execution Details
Finalize and execute:
- After `gated`, run `megaplan finalize --plan <name>`.
- In auto-approve mode, run `megaplan execute --plan <name> --confirm-destructive`.
- In review mode, stop with `BREAKPOINT: EXECUTE_APPROVAL`, then after approval run `megaplan execute --plan <name> --confirm-destructive --user-approved`.

Per-batch execution:
- If the execution briefing or CLI response indicates per-batch execution, continue batch by batch.
- Batch numbering is global and 1-indexed.
- Use `megaplan progress --plan <name>` between batch executions.
- Re-run the same batch number after a timeout if needed; the harness will reconcile previously completed work.

Execution loop end conditions:
- Continue until all actionable tasks are complete and the workflow reaches `done`.
- Stop early only for a defined breakpoint.

## 9. Overrides & Plan Editing
Override rules:
- `megaplan override add-note` is safe from any active state when you need to record new user direction without changing state.
- `megaplan override force-proceed` from `critiqued` moves the run into `gated`. Use it only when the user clearly wants to override the gate.
- `megaplan override force-proceed` cannot bypass a missing project directory or missing success criteria.
- `megaplan override force-proceed` from `executed` moves the run to `done`. Use that only when the user explicitly accepts unresolved review issues.
- `megaplan override abort` ends the run. Use it only when the user clearly wants to stop.
- `megaplan override replan` is available from `critiqued`, `gated`, or `finalized`. Use it when the orchestrator itself needs to edit the plan directly instead of asking the revise worker to do it.

Replan behavior:
- After `override replan`, read the latest plan file, edit it directly, then continue with `critique`.

Step editing:
- `megaplan step add`, `step remove`, and `step move` are available while the run is in `planned`, `critiqued`, `gated`, or `finalized`.
- Use them when you need to insert, remove, or reorder step sections without hand-editing the markdown.
- After a step edit, re-check `status` and continue from the returned state.

## 10. Completion Format
When the workflow completes, return exactly this shape:

`COMPLETE: megaplan run finished`
`Plan: <name>`
`Final state: <state>`
`Summary: <outcome>`
`Artifacts: <key files or reports>`
`Follow-up: <only if something remains>`

## 11. Command Reference
Core commands:
- `megaplan status --plan <name>`
- `megaplan progress --plan <name>`
- `megaplan audit --plan <name>`

Workflow commands:
- `megaplan prep --plan <name>`
- `megaplan plan --plan <name>`
- `megaplan critique --plan <name>`
- `megaplan gate --plan <name>`
- `megaplan revise --plan <name>`
- `megaplan finalize --plan <name>`
- `megaplan execute --plan <name> --confirm-destructive`
- `megaplan execute --plan <name> --confirm-destructive --user-approved`
- `megaplan execute --plan <name> --confirm-destructive --user-approved --batch N`
- `megaplan review --plan <name>`

Override and editing commands:
- `megaplan override add-note --plan <name> --note "..."`
- `megaplan override force-proceed --plan <name> --reason "..."`
- `megaplan override replan --plan <name> --reason "..." [--note "..."]`
- `megaplan override abort --plan <name> --reason "..."`
- `megaplan step add --plan <name> --after S<N> "description"`
- `megaplan step remove --plan <name> S<N>`
- `megaplan step move --plan <name> S<N> --after S<M>`
```

Resume Protocol\nIf resumed via `SendMessage` or relaunched:\n1. Re-read the message carefully.\n2. Run `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan status --plan <name>`.\n3. Read the full `notes` array.\n4. Resume from the current CLI state and `next_step`, not from memory.\n\n## 8. Execution Details\n- After `gated`, run `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan finalize --plan <name>`.\n- Auto-approve mode: run `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan execute --plan <name> --confirm-destructive`.\n- Per-batch if CLI indicates: continue batch by batch, global 1-indexed, use `progress` between batches, re-run same batch number on timeout.\n\n## 9. Overrides & Plan Editing\n- `override add-note` safe from any active state.\n- `override force-proceed` from `critiqued` → `gated`; from `executed` → `done`. Only when user clearly wants override.\n- `override abort` ends the run. Only when user clearly wants to stop.\n- `override replan` from `critiqued`/`gated`/`finalized`: read latest plan, edit directly, continue with `critique`.\n- `step add`/`remove`/`move` in `planned`/`critiqued`/`gated`/`finalized` for section edits.\n\n## 10. Completion Format\n`COMPLETE: megaplan run finished`\n`Plan: <name>`\n`Final state: <state>`\n`Summary: <outcome>`\n`Artifacts: <key files or reports>`\n`Follow-up: <only if something remains>`\n\nBegin by running init now.",
  "subagent_type": "general-purpose",
  "run_in_background": true
}

## Dogfood test
1. **Set up a tiny `cloud.yaml`** pointed at the megaplan repo itself (self-hosted test — you've already got the code) with `provider: railway`, `mode: idle`, `agents.default: codex`, robustness knob unused because the inner plan will set its own.
2. **`megaplan cloud build && megaplan cloud deploy`** — proves the new path end-to-end.
3. **Kick a light-robustness plan inside the container** via `megaplan cloud exec`:
   ```
   megaplan init --project-dir /workspace/megaplan --robustness light \
     "Clean up documentation so it's succinct, easy to understand, and gives the appropriate amount of coverage. No fluff, no marketing voice, no hollow reassurances. Prune anything a reader wouldn't miss. Preserve accuracy."
   ```
   Light robustness = `plan → critique → revise → finalize → execute`, no prep/gate/review. Fast and cheap. Exactly the right scope for a prose-polish task.
4. **Exercise the new commands along the way:** `cloud status`, `cloud logs`, `cloud resume` if it stalls — so the test covers the surface area, not just one happy path.
5. **Pull back:** once the container commits + pushes the branch, `git fetch && git checkout <branch>` locally. Review the diff before merging to main.

## Two decisions I'll default on unless you say otherwise

- **Scope of doc cleanup:** the whole `docs/` tree + `README.md` + the new `docs/cloud.md` sprint 1 just wrote. If you want it narrower (e.g. just `docs/cloud.md` since that's freshly authored and most likely to need a polish pass), say so now and I'll scope the idea text accordingly.
- **Provider:** Railway, because that's what sprint 1 builds. Local/ssh providers aren't until sprint 2. That means the test costs a bit of Railway time + codex API usage. I'd estimate under $5 for a light run on prose — but worth flagging.

## What I'll do when sprint 1 notifies
