Builds on the `megaplan tiebreaker` CLI command being built in a concurrent light-megaplan run (branch `tiebreaker-subcommand`). This run adds the second layer: gate-driven automatic triggering, state machine extension, and all the guardrails. This is the v2 of tiebreaker \u2014 MVP was 'human manually invokes tiebreaker'; v2 is 'harness detects recurrence and routes to tiebreaker automatically with budgeted, audited guardrails.'\n\n## Full spec\n\n**Motivation.** Today, gates can only recommend PROCEED / ITERATE / ESCALATE. When a plan has an unresolvable constraint tension (like M1's RQ-first vs store-first architectural conflict), the only path is ESCALATE \u2014 but the human gets only the gate's rationale, no structured decision context. The MVP tiebreaker CLI produces that context manually. This milestone makes the harness recognize when tiebreaker should run and route automatically \u2014 with enough guardrails that it doesn't burn tokens on false positives.\n\n**Core changes**\n\n### 1. Iteration-pressure signal (compute in evaluation.py / gate_signals.py)\n\nFor each open flag, compute:\n```\n{flag_id, fuzzy_group_id, iterations_open, addressed_then_reopened_count, rationale_similarity_to_prior}\n```\n\n`fuzzy_group_id` is assigned by embedding similarity on the flag's `concern` text \u2014 flags with >0.85 cosine similarity belong to the same fuzzy_group. This catches 'bootstrap race' flags labeled with different IDs across iterations but semantically the same concern. Use a small local embedding model (or an API call if that's already how critique works); make the similarity threshold configurable.\n\n`addressed_then_reopened_count` counts transitions: flag went from `open` to `addressed` and back to `open`. That's the strongest signal of loop failure.\n\nThis table is rendered into the gate prompt context as a plain-text section.\n\n### 2. Extend gate schema and prompt\n\n`megaplan/schemas.py`:\n```python\nGATE_RECOMMENDATION = Literal['PROCEED', 'ITERATE', 'ESCALATE', 'TIEBREAKER']\n\nclass GateOutput(TypedDict):\n    recommendation: GATE_RECOMMENDATION\n    rationale: str\n    flag_resolutions: list[FlagResolution]\n    tiebreaker_question: NotRequired[str]   # required iff recommendation == TIEBREAKER\n    tiebreaker_flag_ids: NotRequired[list[str]]  # which flags this resolves\n    tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id: NotRequired[str]  # which group this resolves\n```\n\n`megaplan/prompts/gate.py` gets a new section:\n> 'If any fuzzy_group shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` OR has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations, consider whether it reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural/philosophical \u2014 requires a human call -> TIEBREAKER) versus a *plan-quality issue* (the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough -> ITERATE). Recommend TIEBREAKER only for the former; cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.'\n\n### 3. Harness validates TIEBREAKER recommendation\n\nIn `handlers.py` `handle_gate`:\n- If gate returns `recommendation: TIEBREAKER`, check that at least one fuzzy_group has `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` OR spans >=2 iterations.\n- If no such group exists, reject the recommendation and re-prompt the gate once with: 'You recommended TIEBREAKER but no flag shows mechanical recurrence signal. Either identify specific flag(s) with iteration history or pick ITERATE.'\n- If re-prompt still says TIEBREAKER without signal, force-demote to ITERATE and log `tiebreaker_rejected_no_signal`.\n\n### 4. New plan states\n\n`megaplan/types.py`:\n```python\nPlanState = Literal[\n    ..., 'critiqued', 'gated', 'finalized', 'executed', 'reviewed', 'done',\n    'tiebreaker_pending',  # NEW \u2014 tiebreaker recommended, researcher/challenger running\n    'tiebreaker_ready',    # NEW \u2014 synthesis done, awaiting human decision\n]\n```\n\nTransitions:\n- `critiqued` -> (gate emits TIEBREAKER, validation passes) -> `tiebreaker_pending`\n- `tiebreaker_pending` -> (tiebreaker runs) -> `tiebreaker_ready`\n- `tiebreaker_ready` -> (human runs `megaplan tiebreaker decide`) -> `critiqued` (with settled flag resolution injected)\n\n### 5. New CLI: `megaplan tiebreaker decide`\n\n```\nmegaplan tiebreaker decide --plan <name> --pick <option_name> --rationale '...'\nmegaplan tiebreaker decide --plan <name> --escalate --rationale '...'  # human chooses to handle outside the tool\nmegaplan tiebreaker decide --plan <name> --replan --rationale '...'    # question itself is wrong\n```\n\nImplementation:\n- Write decision to the plan's `flag_registry` as `settled_by_tiebreaker` against the `fuzzy_group_id`.\n- Record the decision in `tiebreaker_decisions.json` with full context (researcher pick, challenger pick, human pick, rationale, timestamp).\n- Transition state back to `critiqued`. Next revise inherits the settled resolution.\n\n### 6. Settled-decision immunity in critique prompt\n\nIn `megaplan/prompts/critique.py`, add a section listing settled_by_tiebreaker decisions for the current plan. Prompt instruction:\n> 'The following concerns have been settled by human tiebreaker decision. Do NOT re-raise them under a new surface. Only raise a new concern if new evidence *materially* changes the settled premise \u2014 and if so, explicitly cite which settled decision you are challenging and why.'\n\n### 7. Guardrails\n\n- **Budget per plan.** `plan_config.max_tiebreakers_per_plan` (default 2). Counted in the plan's state metadata. Exceeded -> gate's TIEBREAKER force-demoted to ESCALATE with rationale 'tiebreaker budget exhausted'.\n- **Cost ceiling.** Tiebreaker run has a wall-clock (default 30m) and token (default 150k) budget. Exceeded -> abort, emit partial output, force-demote to ESCALATE.\n- **Domain blocklist.** `plan_config.tiebreaker_blocklist: list[str]` (default []). Concerns with category in blocklist skip tiebreaker.\n- **Spec-level opt-out.** Idea file front-matter field `allow_tiebreaker: false` disables the entire mechanism for the plan.\n- **Tiebreaker refusal.** Researcher can emit `decision_not_ripe: true` with reason; harness treats as abort without consuming a tiebreaker budget slot.\n\n### 8. Audit tracking\n\n`megaplan/audit.py` (new or extend existing):\n- After each tiebreaker, record: {plan_name, tiebreaker_index, fuzzy_group_id, question, researcher_pick, challenger_pick, human_pick, matched_researcher, matched_challenger, time_to_decide_seconds, tokens_spent, subsequent_plan_reached_done}.\n- New CLI: `megaplan tiebreaker audit [--plan X | --global]` \u2014 shows usage stats, hit rate, token cost. --global aggregates across all plans.\n\n### 9. Tests\n\n- Unit: iteration_pressure computes correctly across synthetic flag histories.\n- Unit: gate schema validation rejects TIEBREAKER without tiebreaker_question + tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id.\n- Unit: handle_gate rejects TIEBREAKER without mechanical signal, re-prompts, force-demotes if re-prompt fails.\n- Unit: budget enforcement (3rd tiebreaker recommendation on same plan demotes).\n- Unit: settled-decision blocks critique from re-raising same fuzzy_group concern.\n- Integration: synthetic plan with recurring flags runs through critique -> gate -> tiebreaker_pending -> tiebreaker_ready -> decide -> critiqued with settled resolution.\n\n### 10. Out of scope\n\n- Cross-plan tiebreaker detection. Fuzzy-grouping is plan-local; cross-plan is v3.\n- Automatic tiebreaker resolution. Two crons staggered to fire every 90 minutes:

- **Cron A** (`6eba5eaa`): fires at :17 past hours 0, 3, 6, 9, 12, 15, 18, 21
- **Cron B** (`258d7428`): fires at :47 past hours 1, 4, 7, 10, 13, 16, 19, 22

Gap between any two consecutive fires = exactly 90 minutes.

**What each check-in does:**
1. `megaplan status` + `ps` + `TaskList` to read reality.
2. If a subagent task or real worker process is running → do nothing, wait.
3. If idle but state is intermediate (finalized/executed/etc) → relaunch a resume-subagent in the background to drive next_step forward. No re-running of earlier phases.
4. If `done` → auto-delete both crons and **immediately kick off the doc-cleanup dogfood test** you asked for (cloud.yaml on megaplan repo, build + deploy, light-robustness plan cleaning up docs).
5. If `aborted`/`failed` → surface to you and stop.

**Constraints baked in:**
- Session-only (dies if I restart) — flag if you want durable.
- Auto-expire after 7 days.
- 3-attempt no-progress cap on execute → breakpoint instead of burning money.
- Retry-once-with-fresh on non-execute phase failures.

 ## Doc mode — planning documents instead of code
 
-Megaplan has two output modes, picked at `init` with `--mode`:
-
-- `--mode code` (default) — the run produces a code diff.
-- `--mode doc` — the run produces a single document artifact at `--output <path>` (design spec, architecture doc, research note, RFC, proposal, post-mortem, migration plan — anything whose deliverable is prose, not code).
+Pick the output mode at `init` with `--mode`: `--mode code` (default) produces a code diff; `--mode doc` produces a single document artifact at `--output <path>` (design spec, architecture doc, research note, RFC, proposal, post-mortem, migration plan — anything whose deliverable is prose, not code).
 
 ```bash
 megaplan init --project-dir . --mode doc --output docs/new-cache-layer.md \
   "Design a two-tier cache for the ingest pipeline"
 ```
 
-Doc mode runs the same `prep → plan → critique → gate → revise → finalize → execute → review` loop and respects every other flag (`--robustness`, `--auto-approve`, `--phase-model`, subagent mode, overrides). Under the hood it uses authoring-focused prep/execute/review prompts and a section-based execute schema (`sections_written`) instead of per-file changes.
+Doc mode respects every other flag (`--robustness`, `--auto-approve`, `--phase-model`, subagent mode, overrides) and uses authoring-focused prep/execute/review prompts plus a section-based execute schema (`sections_written`) instead of per-file changes.
 
-A common two-step pattern: first run `--mode doc` to produce a rigorous design document, then run `--mode code` against an idea that references that document to implement it.
+A common pattern is to run `--mode doc` first, then `--mode code` against an idea that references that document.
 
 ### Looking for "metaplan" or "preplan" mode?
 
-There is no mode called `metaplan`, `preplan`, `meta-plan`, `pre-plan`, or `design-document mode`. If you want to plan *before* you code — a design-first / preplan workflow — use `--mode doc`. Note that `prep` is the visible repository-investigation *phase* inside every run (both code and doc mode have a prep phase); it is not a separate mode.
+There is no `metaplan`, `preplan`, `meta-plan`, `pre-plan`, or `design-document mode`. For a design-first / preplan workflow, use `--mode doc`; `prep` is the visible repository-investigation *phase* inside every run, not a separate mode.
 
 ## Using different models per phase
 
@@ -112,12 +105,11 @@ GEMINI_API_KEY=...         # for google: prefix

 ```
 
-`status` is the single monitoring command. It exposes lifecycle fields such as `active_step`, `last_step`, notes, cost, execute progress, and next-step runtime guidance.
-`watch` remains as a backward-compatible alias to `status`.
+Use `status` to monitor `active_step`, `last_step`, notes, cost, execute progress, and next-step runtime guidance (`watch` remains a backward-compatible alias).
 
 ## Subagent mode (Claude Code / Codex)
 
-Subagent mode delegates the full workflow to an autonomous agent, returning control only at defined breakpoints. It is the default orchestration mode for Claude Code and Codex. Cursor continues to run inline.
+Subagent mode delegates the full workflow to an autonomous agent and returns control only at defined breakpoints. It is the default orchestration mode for Claude Code and Codex; Cursor continues to run inline.

Three things to flag, all gaps:

### 1. `.env` is NOT gitignored
```
__pycache__/
.claude/
.megaplan/
.desloppify/
.doubledip/
.DS_Store
dist/
*.egg-info/
activity-summary-*.md
megaplan/cloud/_reference/
```
No `.env` pattern. No `.env` file exists in the tree right now, but the cloud feature encourages users to set `OPENAI_API_KEY` / `GITHUB_TOKEN` / `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` — a `.env` would be a natural place to put them. Worth adding `.env` and `.env.*` to `.gitignore` preemptively.

### 2. README.md has zero mentions of "cloud"
The cloud feature is undiscoverable from the main entry point. Anyone landing on the README has no signal that `megaplan cloud` exists.

### 3. The skill (`megaplan/data/instructions.md`) has zero mentions of "cloud"
Agents loaded with the megaplan skill don't know the subcommand exists. They won't suggest it, won't use it, won't answer questions about it. This is probably the bigger gap — the skill is how Claude Code / Codex learn megaplan's capabilities.

### Only `docs/cloud.md` has coverage
192-line standalone doc. Fine as reference, but nothing points to it.

---

Suggested fixes (can do any or all):
1. Add `.env` + `.env.*` + `*.env` to `.gitignore`.
2. Add a short "Cloud runs" section to README.md (3–5 lines + link to `docs/cloud.md`).
3. Add a "Cloud mode" section to `megaplan/data/instructions.md` so the skill teaches it.

Want me to do all three in one commit?

```
## Cloud Mode
`megaplan cloud` runs a plan inside a provider-managed container with a persistent workspace volume, so the run survives the user's terminal session. Suggest it for long-running plans that would outlast a local session or when the user wants an isolated persistent sandbox. Sprint 1 ships the `railway` provider only; `ssh` and `local` are planned.
Subcommands: `init`, `build`, `deploy`, `status`, `attach`, `logs`, `exec`, `resume`, `down`, `destroy`.
Typical flow: `megaplan cloud init` scaffolds `cloud.yaml`; edit it; export the secrets it lists; `megaplan cloud deploy`; then use `status`, `logs`, and `attach` to observe.
See `docs/cloud.md` for the full reference, including `cloud.yaml` fields, mode behavior (`auto`/`chain`/`idle`), secret handling, and troubleshooting.
```

The section is 5 lines of content (plus heading), matches the file's terse directive tone, lists all 10 subcommands, states when to suggest cloud mode, points to `docs/cloud.md` for detail, and leaves every other section untouched.

```
1	# Megaplan
2	
3	A planning and execution harness for structured phases — plan, critique, gate, revise, finalize, execute, and review — with independent critique and gating instead of one-shot attempts.
4	
5	## Quick Start
6	
7	Claude Code / Codex:
8	
9	```
10	Please install megaplan and set it up for this project:
11	
12	pip install megaplan-harness
13	megaplan setup
14	
15	Once you're done, ask me what I need megaplan for.
16	```
17	
18	OpenRouter / open models:
19	
20	```
21	Please install megaplan with the open-model backend and set it up:
22	
23	pip install megaplan-harness hermes-agent
24	
25	Then create ~/.hermes/.env with:
26	OPENROUTER_API_KEY=<my key>
27	
28	Then run: megaplan setup
29	
30	Once you're done, ask me what I need megaplan for.
31	```
32	
33	Get an OpenRouter key at [openrouter.ai/keys](https://openrouter.ai/keys). Any model on OpenRouter works.
34	
35	## How it works
36	
37	```
38	plan → critique → gate → [revise → critique → gate]* → finalize → execute → review
39	```
40	
41	Each phase can use a different model. Independent critique and gating prevent rubber-stamping, and the visible `prep` phase makes repository investigation observable instead of hiding it inside `plan`.
42	
43	Run the phases manually with:
44	
45	```bash
46	megaplan init --project-dir . "Fix the authentication bug in login.py"
47	megaplan plan --plan <name>
48	megaplan critique --plan <name>
49	megaplan gate --plan <name>
50	megaplan finalize --plan <name>
51	megaplan execute --plan <name>
52	```
53	
54	## Doc mode — planning documents instead of code
55	
56	Pick the output mode at `init` with `--mode`: `--mode code` (default) produces a code diff; `--mode doc` produces a single document artifact at `--output <path>` (design spec, architecture doc, research note, RFC, proposal, post-mortem, migration plan — anything whose deliverable is prose, not code).
57	
58	```bash
59	megaplan init --project-dir . --mode doc --output docs/new-cache-layer.md \
60	  "Design a two-tier cache for the ingest pipeline"
61	```
62	
63	Doc mode respects every other flag (`--robustness`, `--auto-approve`, `--phase-model`, subagent mode, overrides) and uses authoring-focused prep/execute/review prompts plus a section-based execute schema (`sections_written`) instead of per-file changes.
64	
65	A common pattern is to run `--mode doc` first, then `--mode code` against an idea that references that document.
66	
67	### Looking for "metaplan" or "preplan" mode?
68	
69	There is no `metaplan`, `preplan`, `meta-plan`, `pre-plan`, or `design-document mode`. For a design-first / preplan workflow, use `--mode doc`; `prep` is the visible repository-investigation *phase* inside every run, not a separate mode.
70	
71	## Using different models per phase
72	
73	Models with provider prefixes route to direct APIs. Models without a prefix go through OpenRouter:
74	
75	```json
76	{
77	  "models": {
78	    "prep": "zhipu:glm-5.1",
79	    "plan": "zhipu:glm-5.1",
80	    "critique": "minimax:MiniMax-M2.7-highspeed",
81	    "execute": "zhipu:glm-5.1",
82	    "review": "minimax:MiniMax-M2.7-highspeed"
83	  }
84	}
85	```
86	
87	Configure direct provider keys in `~/.hermes/.env`:
88	
89	```bash
90	ZHIPU_API_KEY=...          # for zhipu: prefix
91	MINIMAX_API_KEY=...        # for minimax: prefix
92	GEMINI_API_KEY=...         # for google: prefix
93	```
94	
95	## Robustness levels
96	
97	- **light** — visible `prep` + one critique/revise pass, no gate or review
98	- **standard** — visible `prep` + 4 critique checks (default)
99	- **robust** — visible `prep` + 8 critique checks + parallel critique
100	- **superrobust** — same as robust + parallel review
101	
102	## Observability
103	
104	```bash
105	megaplan status --plan <name>
106	```
107	
108	Use `status` to monitor `active_step`, `last_step`, notes, cost, execute progress, and next-step runtime guidance (`watch` remains a backward-compatible alias).
109	
110	## Cloud runs
111	
112	`megaplan cloud` deploys a plan to a remote runner backed by a persistent workspace volume. Sprint 1 ships the `railway` provider only; `ssh` and `local` providers are planned. See [docs/cloud.md](docs/cloud.md) for `cloud.yaml` fields, secrets handling, and the manual-upload workflow for idea/chain files.
113	
114	```bash
115	megaplan cloud init       # scaffold cloud.yaml
116	megaplan cloud deploy     # upload secrets and launch the runner
117	```
118	
119	## Subagent mode (Claude Code / Codex)
120	
121	Subagent mode delegates the full workflow to an autonomous agent and returns control only at defined breakpoints. It is the default orchestration mode for Claude Code and Codex; Cursor continues to run inline.
122	
123	```bash
124	megaplan config set orchestration.mode subagent   # default
125	megaplan config set orchestration.mode inline      # switch back
126	```
127	
128	## Configuration & Defaults
129	
130	View all settings with `megaplan config show`. Override with `megaplan config set <key> <value>`. Reset with `megaplan config reset`.
131	
132	| Key | Default | Description |
133	|-----|---------|-------------|
134	| `orchestration.mode` | `subagent` | `inline` or `subagent` (Claude Code and Codex) |
135	| `orchestration.max_critique_concurrency` | `2` | Max parallel critique checks |
136	| `execution.worker_timeout_seconds` | `7200` | Worker process timeout (seconds) |
137	| `execution.max_execute_no_progress` | `3` | No-progress execute attempts before escalation |
138	| `execution.max_review_rework_cycles` | `3` | Review→rework loops before force-proceeding |
139	| `agents.<step>` | varies | Agent for each phase (`claude`, `codex`, `hermes`) |
140	
141	```bash
142	megaplan config set execution.worker_timeout_seconds 3600
143	megaplan config set agents.critique hermes
144	megaplan config reset
145	```
146	
147	## SWE-bench Experiment
148	
149	Megaplan is being tested live against Claude 4.5 Opus on SWE-bench Verified:
150	
151	- **[Live dashboard](https://peteromallet.github.io/swe-bench-challenge/)** — watch the experiment in real time
152	- **[hermes-megaplan](https://github.com/peteromallet/hermes-megaplan)** — experiment orchestration code
153	
154	## Code Health
155	
156	<img src="scorecard.png" width="100%">
157	
158	## License
159	
160	MIT
161

Honest look at what's bloating it:

### Biggest wins (top 3)

**1. Quick Start is 29 lines of agent-prompts to copy-paste.** For a README, that's a lot. Most new readers don't want to be told "give this to your agent" — they want the commands. Collapse to ~8 lines:
```
pip install megaplan-harness
megaplan setup
megaplan init --project-dir . "fix the auth bug in login.py"
```
Push the "agent bootstrap prompt" variant to a single line: *"In Claude Code or Codex? Paste the install line; `megaplan setup` writes the skill."* OpenRouter variant becomes a 3-line note.

**2. "Looking for metaplan/preplan?" subsection (3 lines).** This is defensive SEO against a confused reader. It's useful for agents, but readable only by someone who already typed the wrong thing. Move it into the `docs/` as an FAQ entry, or drop it entirely — the doc-mode section already explains what exists.

**3. Manual `megaplan init/plan/critique/...` command list in "How it works" (7 lines).** Nobody drives it manually anymore; auto-approve + subagent mode is the real path. Show one `megaplan init` line and link further reading. Saves 6 lines.

### Smaller wins

**4. Subagent mode paragraph has two redundant sentences.** "Subagent mode delegates the full workflow to an autonomous agent and returns control only at defined breakpoints. It is the default orchestration mode for Claude Code and Codex..." — merge into one.

**5. Configuration & Defaults has both a table AND duplicate `config set` examples.** Table says it. Trailing code examples (lines 141–145) just repeat config show/set/reset already mentioned at line 130. Cut the trailing block.

**6. Model names in "Using different models per phase" look fabricated** (`zhipu:glm-5.1`, `minimax:MiniMax-M2.7-highspeed`). If they're aspirational, the example is misleading. Replace with real, current models or mark the JSON as illustrative.

### Structural

- `SWE-bench Experiment` near the bottom feels demoted; either move it near the top (it's a credibility anchor) or leave it as a footer.
- `Code Health` with an `<img>` is a 1-line section — either commit the image to the repo (is it?) or remove.

### Approximate shrinkage if all applied

151 → **~100 lines**, with no information loss. The content that stays lands harder because the surrounding noise is gone.

Quick clarifying call: I can do the README rename now, but `--mode doc` is still the CLI flag in code. If we say "metaplan mode" in docs but users type `--mode metaplan` and get an error, that's a real foot-gun. Three paths — tell me which and I'll run it:

**A. README-only rename (5 min).** Section says "Metaplan mode — produces planning documents." CLI examples still show `--mode doc`. Clear explanation but readers may try `--mode metaplan` and hit an error.

**B. Full rename, `metaplan` as canonical, `doc` kept as alias (30–60 min).** README + skill instructions + CLI accepts both; internal representation stays `doc` so state files and tests don't need changes. Cleanest user-facing naming, no backward-compat risk.

**C. Full rename everywhere, `doc` deprecated (bigger).** Touches CLI, schemas, prompts, tests, state-file reads. More invasive than B and not clearly better unless you want `doc` eventually removed.

My pick: **B**. You get the user-facing name you want, and old commands still work.

Assuming B — shall I proceed? I'll also remove the SWE-bench section in the same commit. The "Looking for metaplan/preplan?" defensive subsection gets merged into the main Metaplan mode section as a one-line note (since the defensive framing becomes obsolete when the name is real).

For a design-first / preplan workflow, use `--mode doc`; `prep` is the visible repository-investigation *phase* inside every run, not a separate mode.",
  "new_string": "## Metaplan mode — planning documents instead of code\n\nMetaplan mode produces a single document artifact — design spec, architecture doc, research note, RFC, proposal, post-mortem, migration plan — instead of a code diff. Pick it at `init` with `--mode metaplan` (or `--mode doc`, the original flag name kept as an alias) and `--output <path>`:\n\n```bash\nmegaplan init --project-dir . --mode metaplan --output docs/new-cache-layer.md \\\n  \"Design a two-tier cache for the ingest pipeline\"\n```\n\nIt respects every other flag (`--robustness`, `--auto-approve`, `--phase-model`, subagent mode, overrides) and uses authoring-focused prep/execute/review prompts plus a section-based execute schema (`sections_written`) instead of per-file changes. A common pattern is to run metaplan mode first, then `--mode code` against an idea that references the resulting document.\n\nNote: `prep` is a visible repository-investigation *phase* inside every run, not a separate mode."
}

15	- **Heavy**: high-stakes changes where getting it wrong is expensive — security-critical code, data migrations, public API changes. Uses the same visible `prep` phase but with 8 critique checks instead of 4.
16	
17	Default to standard unless the task is clearly simple enough for light. Do not ask the user to choose robustness — pick it yourself based on the above. Only ask execution mode (auto-approve or review) when using megaplan.
18	## Modes
19	Megaplan has two output modes, picked with `--mode` at `init`:
20	- **`--mode code`** (default): the run produces a code diff. Execute workers emit per-task file changes. Use for features, refactors, bug fixes, migrations — anything whose deliverable is source code.
21	- **`--mode doc`**: the run produces a single document artifact at `--output <relative/path>` (e.g. `docs/design.md`). The prep, execute, and review phases use authoring-specific prompts; the execute schema uses `sections_written` instead of file changes; auditing reasons about section delivery. Use for design docs, architecture specs, research notes, RFCs, proposals, post-mortems, migration plans — anything whose deliverable is prose, not code.
22	
23	All other flags (`--robustness`, `--auto-approve`, `--phase-model`, `--hermes`, subagent mode, overrides, step editing) behave identically in both modes. The workflow phases are the same: `prep → plan → critique → gate → revise → finalize → execute → review`.
24	
25	A common pattern is two runs: first `--mode doc` to produce a rigorous design document, then `--mode code` on a new idea that references that document to implement it.
26	
27	**`--mode` and `--output` go together.** `init` rejects `--output` without `--mode doc` (error `invalid_args`), and rejects `--mode doc` without `--output`. Don't try to pass one without the other.
28	
29	### Looking for "metaplan" or "preplan" mode?
30	There is no mode called `metaplan`, `preplan`, `meta-plan`, `pre-plan`, or `design-document mode`. If you want to plan the plan — produce a design spec, RFC, or architecture document before writing code — use `--mode doc`. That is the "design-first / preplan" workflow. Note that `prep` is the visible repository-investigation *phase* inside every workflow (both code and doc mode have a prep phase); it is not a mode and is not something you select at `init`.
31	## Start
32	Run `<launcher> config show` before `init`. If `raw_config.execution.auto_approve` is explicitly present, do not ask the execution-mode question and honor that configured override, including configured `false`. If that raw key is absent, ask execution mode (auto-approve or review) before `init`. In the same config check, respect `execution.robustness` as a settable override when it is configured; otherwise pick robustness yourself per the triage guidance above.
33	```bash
34	<launcher> init --project-dir "$PROJECT_DIR" [--auto-approve] [--robustness light|standard|robust|superrobust] [--mode code|doc] [--output docs/foo.md] "$IDEA"
35	```
36	For doc-mode runs, pass `--mode doc --output <relative/path>` (the path is where the final document artifact is written, relative to the project dir). Everything else is identical to code mode.
37	Report the plan name, execution mode, robustness, mode (and `--output` path when doc mode), current state, and next step.
38	## Workflow
39	Run the loop in this order:
40	1. `prep`
41	2. `plan`
42	3. `critique`
43	4. `gate`
44	5. `revise` when gate recommends iteration
45	6. `finalize`
46	7. `execute`
47	8. `review`
48	Use `next_step` and `valid_next` for CLI routing. After `gate`, follow `orchestrator_guidance` instead of manually interpreting gate signals. When a response includes `next_step_runtime`, use its `duration_hint` and `recommended_next_check_seconds` to calibrate timing.
49	At `--robustness light`, the loop is: `plan` → `critique` → `revise` → `finalize` → `execute`. There is no prep, no gate, and no review.
```

1	"""Smoke tests for handle_init's doc-mode arg validation (--mode/--output)."""
2	from __future__ import annotations
3	
4	from argparse import Namespace
5	from pathlib import Path
6	
7	import pytest
8	
9	import megaplan
10	import megaplan._core
11	import megaplan._core.io as io_module
12	import megaplan.cli
13	from megaplan.types import CliError
14	
15	
16	def _bootstrap(tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> tuple[Path, Path]:
17	    root = tmp_path / "root"
18	    project_dir = tmp_path / "project"
19	    config_path = tmp_path / "config"
20	    root.mkdir()
21	    project_dir.mkdir()
22	    (project_dir / ".git").mkdir()
23	
24	    def _config_dir(home: Path | None = None) -> Path:
25	        del home
26	        return config_path
27	
28	    monkeypatch.setenv(megaplan.MOCK_ENV_VAR, "1")
29	    monkeypatch.setattr(
30	        megaplan._core.shutil,
31	        "which",
32	        lambda name: "/usr/bin/mock" if name in {"claude", "codex"} else None,
33	    )
34	    monkeypatch.setattr(io_module, "config_dir", _config_dir)
35	    monkeypatch.setattr(megaplan.cli, "config_dir", _config_dir)
36	    return root, project_dir
37	
38	
39	def _args(project_dir: Path, **overrides: object) -> Namespace:
40	    data: dict[str, object] = {
41	        "plan": None,
42	        "idea": "doc-mode test",
43	        "name": None,
44	        "project_dir": str(project_dir),
45	        "auto_approve": None,
46	        "robustness": "standard",
47	        "agent": None,
48	        "mode": "code",
49	        "output": None,
50	        "hermes": None,
51	    }
52	    data.update(overrides)
53	    return Namespace(**data)
54	
55	
56	def test_doc_mode_requires_output(tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
57	    root, project_dir = _bootstrap(tmp_path, monkeypatch)
58	    with pytest.raises(CliError) as info:
59	        megaplan.handle_init(root, _args(project_dir, mode="doc", output=None))
60	    assert info.value.code == "invalid_args"
61	    assert "--output" in str(info.value)
62	
63	
64	def test_output_rejects_absolute_path(tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
65	    root, project_dir = _bootstrap(tmp_path, monkeypatch)
66	    with pytest.raises(CliError) as info:
67	        megaplan.handle_init(
68	            root,
69	            _args(project_dir, mode="doc", output="/etc/passwd"),
70	        )
71	    assert info.value.code == "invalid_args"
72	    assert "relative" in str(info.value).lower() or "absolute" in str(info.value).lower()
73	
74	
75	def test_output_rejects_parent_traversal(tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
76	    root, project_dir = _bootstrap(tmp_path, monkeypatch)
77	    with pytest.raises(CliError) as info:
78	        megaplan.handle_init(
79	            root,
80	            _args(project_dir, mode="doc", output="../escape.md"),
81	        )
82	    assert info.value.code == "invalid_args"
83	    assert ".." in str(info.value)
84	
85	
86	def test_doc_mode_accepts_relative_output(tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
87	    root, project_dir = _bootstrap(tmp_path, monkeypatch)
88	    response = megaplan.handle_init(
89	        root,
90	        _args(project_dir, name="doc-plan", mode="doc", output="docs/result.md"),
91	    )
92	    plan_dir = megaplan.plans_root(root) / response["plan"]
93	    import json as _json

95	    assert state["config"]["mode"] == "doc"
96	    assert state["config"]["output_path"] == "docs/result.md"
97

## Metaplan mode — planning documents instead of code

Metaplan mode produces a single document artifact — design spec, architecture doc, research note, RFC, proposal, post-mortem, migration plan — instead of a code diff. Pick it at `init` with `--mode metaplan` (or `--mode doc`, the original flag name kept as an alias) and `--output <path>`:

```bash
megaplan init --project-dir . --mode metaplan --output docs/new-cache-layer.md \
  "Design a two-tier cache for the ingest pipeline"
```

It respects every other flag (`--robustness`, `--auto-approve`, `--phase-model`, subagent mode, overrides) and uses authoring-focused prep/execute/review prompts plus a section-based execute schema (`sections_written`) instead of per-file changes. A common pattern is to run metaplan mode first, then `--mode code` against an idea that references the resulting document.

Note: `prep` is a visible repository-investigation *phase* inside every run, not a separate mode.

## Using different models per phase

Models with provider prefixes route to direct APIs. Models without a prefix go through OpenRouter:
```

# Changelog

## v0.18.1 — 2026-04-16

### Rework-cycle-aware stall detection in `megaplan auto`

Fixes a false-positive stall in the auto-driver when a plan is in a review→rework loop.

- **Bug**: `megaplan auto` counted iterations at the same `state` and bailed after `--stall-threshold` (default 5). When review returns `needs_rework`, state ping-pongs `finalized ↔ executed ↔ finalized` while execute re-runs batches. From the naive stall counter's view the plan looked "stuck at finalized for 5 iterations" even though new `review.json` / `execution.json` artifacts were being written and real progress was happening. Observed on plan `milestone-m1b-from-docs-state-20260416-1226` — 7 execute batches + 1 review cycle completed, driver exited rc=2.
- **Fix**: auto's driver loop now tracks `review.json` mtime as a forward-progress marker. When the marker advances, the stall counter resets and a rework-cycle counter increments. The new `--max-review-rework-cycles` flag (default 3, mirroring `execution.max_review_rework_cycles`) caps runaway rework loops independently of `--stall-threshold`.
- **Helpers**: `_resolve_plan_dir(plan, cwd)` walks up from cwd to find `.megaplan/plans/<plan>`, matching how `megaplan status` resolves plans. `_get_review_marker(plan_dir)` stats `review.json` and returns `None` for plans without a review artifact (e.g. light-robustness plans) — in that case the driver falls back to plain stall detection, preserving existing behavior.
- **CLI**: `--stall-threshold` help text updated to clarify that it fires only when state AND `review.json` are both unchanged. New `--max-review-rework-cycles` flag added with matching default.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_auto.py` (new file) covers stall reset on review-marker advance, rework cap enforcement, plain-stall fallback when no review.json exists, and the plan-dir resolver.
- **Scope**: auto.py driver loop only — execute and review phase logic are untouched.

## v0.18.0 — 2026-04-15

### Automated tiebreaker triggering

Gate-driven automatic tiebreaker routing with budgeted, audited guardrails. Builds on the advisory `megaplan tiebreaker` subcommand from v0.17.1 — the harness now detects recurring constraint tensions and routes to tiebreaker automatically.

- **Iteration-pressure analysis**: `megaplan/iteration_pressure.py` computes flag recurrence history — fuzzy-groups flags by Jaccard word similarity, tracks `addressed_then_reopened_count`, and renders a pressure table into the gate prompt context.
- **Mechanical recurrence validation**: harness validates TIEBREAKER recommendations against actual flag history via `_validate_tiebreaker`. Re-prompts the gate once if no mechanical signal exists; force-demotes to ITERATE on second failure.
- **`tiebreaker-run` top-level command**: auto-driver-callable command that invokes `_run_tiebreaker` inside a plan-locked context and transitions the plan state.
- **Gate integration**: `handle_gate` detects `TIEBREAKER` recommendations, runs the validator, transitions to `tiebreaker_pending` on approval.
- **Budget guardrails**: `max_tiebreakers_per_plan` (default 2). Exceeded budgets force-demote to ESCALATE.
- **Domain blocklist**: `tiebreaker_blocklist` config field skips tiebreaker for specified concern categories.
- **Spec-level opt-out**: `allow_tiebreaker: false` in config disables the mechanism entirely.
- **Audit tracking**: `megaplan/audit.py` records tiebreaker usage, timing, and token costs. `megaplan tiebreaker audit` CLI for per-plan and global stats. `handle_tiebreaker_decide` writes an audit record on every decision.
- **Auto driver**: `drive()` returns `status="awaiting_human"`, `"tiebreaker_pending"`, or `"tiebreaker_ready"` for the matching automation-terminal states and stops cleanly.
- **Gate prompt**: now includes the Iteration Pressure Analysis block; TIEBREAKER bullet explicitly cites `addressed_then_reopened_count` thresholds.

## v0.17.1 — 2026-04-15

### Tiebreaker subcommand (`megaplan tiebreaker`)

New advisory subcommand that produces structured decision context for architectural questions (e.g. when `gate` returns ESCALATE).

- **Two-agent pipeline**: a researcher agent gathers evidence and options, then a challenger agent stress-tests the findings — each in an independent ephemeral session.
- **CLI**: `megaplan tiebreaker --plan <name> --question "..."` (or `--question-file`), `megaplan tiebreaker status`, `megaplan tiebreaker decide --pick/--escalate/--replan --rationale`.
```

```markdown
## v0.19.0 — 2026-04-21

### Built-in cloud runner via `megaplan cloud`

Generalises the ad-hoc Railway container setup (previously external, at `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/`) into a first-class subcommand. Sprint 1 of two; scope is operational parity with the existing Railway flow.

- **New subcommand group**: `megaplan cloud {init,build,deploy,status,attach,logs,exec,resume,down,destroy}` covers the full lifecycle of a provider-backed run.
- **Subpackage layout** under `megaplan/cloud/`: `spec.py` (cloud.yaml loader + schema validation), `template.py` (renders `entrypoint.sh` / `railway.toml` / `cloud.yaml`), `cli.py` (argparse wiring, dispatched from `megaplan.cli`).
- **Provider plugin model**: `providers/` ships a `Provider` ABC plus a Railway plugin that wraps the `railway` CLI. Non-Railway providers (ssh, local) are explicit non-goals for this sprint.
- **Bundled templates**: `megaplan/cloud/templates/` ships `Dockerfile`, `entrypoint.sh.tmpl`, `healthserver.py`, `railway.toml.tmpl`, `cloud.yaml.tmpl`, and `chain.yaml.example`.
- **Extracted run wrappers**: `megaplan/cloud/wrappers/` ships `mp-run`, `mp-supervise`, `mp-heartbeat`, and `mp-chain` bash scripts, lifted out of the old entrypoint heredocs so they are versioned and editable.
- **`handle_init` strictness**: `--output` on a code-mode plan now raises `invalid_args` instead of being silently accepted. The only legitimate use of `--output` was a dropped `--mode doc` flag, which is now an explicit error.
- **Discoverability**: README gains a short "Cloud runs" section, `megaplan/data/instructions.md` gains a "Cloud Mode" block so the skill teaches Claude Code / Codex when to suggest `megaplan cloud`, and `.gitignore` now covers `.env` / `.env.*` / `*.env` since the cloud flow encourages `OPENAI_API_KEY` / `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` / `GITHUB_TOKEN` secrets.
- **Out of scope for sprint 1**: ssh/local providers, `cloud init-plan` bootstrap, toolchain extensibility block, retiring the external reference folder. The existing `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` keeps running unchanged.

### `--mode metaplan` alias for doc mode

- **CLI**: `megaplan init --mode metaplan` is now the preferred name for design/document runs. Internally it normalizes to `mode == "doc"`, so all existing state files, prompts, schemas, and downstream checks are untouched. `--mode doc` remains a valid alias.
- **Docs**: README section renamed to "Metaplan mode," with the defensive "Looking for metaplan/preplan?" subsection merged in now that metaplan is the real name. The SWE-bench Experiment section was dropped. Skill instructions updated to match.

### Doc-mode aggregator preserves executor output

- **Bug**: `assemble_doc` was overwriting the executor-authored output file with `executor_notes` (verification prose), so every doc-mode run ended with status-summary text instead of the intended deliverable. The executor is the authoritative author — the aggregator's only job is to fall back when the executor couldn't write to disk.
- **Fix**: `assemble_doc` now returns untouched if the output file exists with non-empty content. Empty or missing files still fall through to the degraded notes-based path; a docstring callout flags that its content is verification prose, not authored sections. Regression tests cover both branches.

### Misc docs

- **Doc-mode / metaplan pointer**: pre-alias, v0.15.0's doc mode wasn't surfaced in the skill or README, and users searching for "metaplan" / "preplan" weren't landing on it. README got a "Doc mode" section, `instructions.md` got a Modes block with a keyword-loaded "Looking for metaplan or preplan mode?" subsection, and `claude_subagent_appendix.md` notes the `--mode doc --output` flags the outer skill appends.
- **README prose cleanup**: light-robustness megaplan run, prose-only; compresses quick-start, how-it-works, doc-mode, and subagent sections without altering factual claims or code examples.
- **License correction**: README had claimed MIT; actual license per `LICENSE` and `pyproject.toml` is Open Source Native License (OSNL) 0.2. README now carries a short accurate summary and links to `LICENSE`.
```

**ARTIFACT 2: GitHub release body**

```markdown
## megaplan v0.19.0

### Highlights

- **`megaplan cloud`** — new built-in subcommand for provider-agnostic cloud runs. Generalises the ad-hoc Railway container setup into a first-class feature with its own lifecycle commands and a pluggable provider model (Railway ships; ssh/local to follow).
- **`--mode metaplan`** — design/document runs now have a proper name. Alias for `--mode doc`, normalized internally so all existing plans keep working.
- **Doc-mode output fix** — aggregator no longer clobbers the file the executor just wrote.

### `megaplan cloud` quick start

```bash
# One-time setup for a project
megaplan cloud init

# Build image, deploy to Railway, tail logs
megaplan cloud build
megaplan cloud deploy
megaplan cloud logs

# Attach to the running container, or exec into it
megaplan cloud attach
megaplan cloud exec -- bash

# Tear down when done
megaplan cloud down
```

Full lifecycle: `init`, `build`, `deploy`, `status`, `attach`, `logs`, `exec`, `resume`, `down`, `destroy`. See [`docs/cloud.md`](docs/cloud.md) for the complete reference, spec schema, and provider plugin details.

### Other changes

- `megaplan init --output` on a code-mode plan is now a hard error (was silently accepted) — it only ever made sense with `--mode doc`.
- `.gitignore` now covers `.env` / `.env.*` / `*.env`; the cloud flow encourages API-key secrets.
- README tightened; license corrected to OSNL 0.2 (was incorrectly claimed as MIT); SWE-bench Experiment section dropped.

Full notes: [`CHANGELOG.md`](CHANGELOG.md).
```

- I used "first-class subcommand" in the release body but otherwise avoided marketing language. If "first-class" reads as marketing to you, swap for "built-in."
- The release body links to `docs/cloud.md` per your instruction; I didn't verify that path exists in the repo — if it lives elsewhere, adjust the link.

> AGENT

Good news — **chain is preserved**, not missing. Let me show what shipped and how it's meant to be used.

### What sprint 1 actually shipped for chain

| Artifact | What it does |
|---|---|
| `megaplan/cloud/templates/chain.yaml.example` | Example spec with `seed`, `milestones`, `on_failure`, `on_escalate`, `driver` sections — same shape as reigh's |
| `megaplan/cloud/wrappers/mp-chain` | Bash wrapper that invokes `megaplan chain --spec ...` |
| `cloud.yaml` spec: `mode: chain` + `chain.spec: <path>` | Picks chain mode and points at the uploaded spec |
| Entrypoint wiring | `mode: chain` launches `mp-supervise chain mp-chain <spec>`; missing spec warns and drops to idle tmux |
| `docs/cloud.md` | Covers the manual upload workflow |

The underlying capability is still `megaplan chain` (in `megaplan/chain.py`) — cloud just packages it to run inside a container, same as reigh did.

### Current flow for running a chain in the cloud

```bash
# 1. Local: write chain.yaml describing your milestones
# 2. Edit cloud.yaml: mode: chain, chain.spec: /workspace/chain.yaml
megaplan cloud init
# 3. Deploy the runner
megaplan cloud deploy
# 4. Upload the chain spec to the running container
cat chain.yaml | megaplan cloud exec "cat > /workspace/chain.yaml"
# 5. Restart so entrypoint picks up the spec
megaplan cloud deploy
```

Step 4's manual upload is an explicit sprint-1 parity decision — reigh did it the same way. Sprint 2 is slated to add `cloud init-plan` which would collapse this into one command.

### "How should these capabilities be applied?" — a framework

**Use chain when:**
- You have **pre-decomposable** milestones (M1 → M1a → M1b) where each has a written idea file up front.
- Each milestone is **independently specifiable** — its scope doesn't depend on what the previous milestone produced.
- You want **unattended overnight runs** that keep advancing without a human at the keyboard.

Reigh's state-management refactor fit this perfectly: 4 weeks of pre-planned milestones (M0 docs, M1 foundation store, M1a settings store, M1b selection store, M1c timeline store) all decomposed before kick-off.

**Don't use chain when:**
- Later work **depends on reviewing** earlier output. This is why we're doing sprint 1 and sprint 2 as separate plans, not a chain. Sprint 2's scope sharpens after we see what sprint 1 actually built.
- Scope is **exploratory** — if you can't write the idea file now, chain can't run it later.

### What's missing from sprint 1 that would make chain nicer

- **No `megaplan cloud chain <spec.yaml>` convenience command.** Today it's: `cloud init` → edit yaml → `cloud deploy` → upload → redeploy. A one-liner that takes the chain spec directly, sets `mode: chain`, uploads, and deploys would collapse four steps into one. Good sprint 2 candidate.
- **No `megaplan cloud status` milestone awareness.** `cloud status` returns the active plan's status, but a chain has N plans. A `--chain` flag could surface `current_milestone`, `completed`, `remaining`. Also sprint 2.
- **No `cloud init-plan` shortcut for single-plan auto mode.** Same manual-upload workflow applies to `mode: auto`. Deferred to sprint 2 by explicit scope decision.

Want me to add any of these to the sprint 2 brief when we get there? Or open issues for them now so they don't get lost?

> DEVELOPER

Should that be a cloud capability or a megaplan one we can use in the cloud?

> AGENT

**Core megaplan, with the cloud as a thin transport layer.** That's the pattern sprint 1 already established: `megaplan cloud status` doesn't reinvent a parser — it just runs `megaplan status` remotely and streams the same JSON back.

### The principle

Ask: *"If I ran this command on my laptop against a local plan, would it be useful?"*

- **Yes** → belongs in core megaplan. Cloud wraps it by invoking it remotely.
- **No, it only makes sense when something is deployed somewhere** → belongs in cloud.

Applied to the three chain gaps:

| Gap | Where it belongs | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Chain spec → running-plan one-liner | **Core**: `megaplan chain start --spec <path>` (maybe with `--auto`). Cloud adds a wrapper that uploads the spec first, then calls it. | Local users running chain don't need to do `init → auto` separately; they'd benefit from the same convenience. |
| Milestone-aware status | **Core**: `megaplan chain status --spec <path>` reads `chain_state.json`. Cloud's `cloud status --chain` fetches the remote file and calls the same formatter. | Chain state exists locally too. Reinventing it in cloud would mean two parsers to keep in sync. |
| Single-plan idea bootstrap | **Core**: `megaplan init --idea-file <path>` + optional `--auto-start` flag. Cloud adds the upload step. | "Read idea from a file and kick off auto" has zero cloud-specific semantics. |

### What cloud *should* own

- Lifecycle: `build`, `deploy`, `down`, `destroy`, `attach`, `logs` — no local analogue.
- Transport: uploading files, fetching remote state, provider-specific quirks (Railway `variables --set`, ssh tunnelling).
- Secrets handling: env-var injection, rotation reminders. Doesn't belong in core because a local run just uses your shell env.

### Why this matters for sprint 2 planning

If we put these in cloud, we fork the user experience: local chain users get a worse product than cloud chain users for no reason. If we put them in core, every improvement pays dividends in both places, and cloud gets simpler.

Concretely: sprint 2's scope should probably include a small core-megaplan chapter (`chain start`, `chain status`, `init --idea-file`) that cloud wraps, not a pile of `cloud chain *` subcommands. That's a real shift from my earlier framing — want me to update the sprint 2 brief accordingly?

> DEVELOPER

Yes please

> AGENT

Updated sprint 2 brief below, and I'll save the underlying architecture principle to memory so it doesn't get lost across sessions.

## Sprint 2 — core ergonomics, more providers, retire the reigh folder

**Shift from the original sketch:** chain/init improvements move into core megaplan first; cloud becomes a thin wrapper over them instead of growing its own parallel surface.

### Week 3 — core first

**Day 11 — `megaplan chain start --spec <path>`** *(core)*
Validates the spec, resolves referenced idea files, kicks the auto-loop per milestone. Replaces the current workflow of "init + auto" pair. Works against a local spec with zero cloud dependency.

**Day 12 — `megaplan chain status --spec <path>`** *(core)*
Reads `chain_state.json`, pretty-prints `current_milestone`, `completed`, `remaining`, per-milestone status. Unit tests against synthetic chain states.

**Day 13 — `megaplan init --idea-file <path>` + `--auto-start`** *(core)*
"Read idea from a file, create the plan, optionally kick auto immediately." One command where today you write `init "<idea>"` then `auto`. Useful locally, essential for cloud bootstrap.

**Day 14 — Cloud wrappers now thin** *(cloud)*
- `megaplan cloud chain <spec.yaml>` = upload spec + `cloud exec` to call `megaplan chain start` remotely
- `megaplan cloud bootstrap <idea-file>` = upload idea + `cloud exec` to call `megaplan init --idea-file --auto-start` remotely
- `megaplan cloud status --chain` = fetch remote `chain_state.json` + reuse the core formatter

**Day 15 — Local provider (docker compose)** *(cloud)*
Fastest iteration target for the whole sprint. No Railway costs, no railway CLI needed. Also becomes the primary provider for CI smoke tests.

### Week 4 — generalisation + retirement

**Day 16 — SSH provider** *(cloud)*
Plain docker-over-ssh. "Any host" story lands.

**Day 17 — Toolchain extensibility** *(cloud)*
`toolchains:` block in `cloud.yaml` with recipes for `rust`, `go`, `java`, `custom`. Renders extra Dockerfile stages. Turns "any folder" into a real claim instead of "Python/Node folder."

**Day 18 — Fly provider** *(optional, skip if time-pressured)*

**Day 19 — Hardening**
Secret redaction in logs, idempotency contract documented, graceful fail when `railway`/`docker` CLI missing, better error messages on spec validation.

**Day 20 — Parity test + retire reigh-megaplan-dev**
Deploy cloud with a `cloud.yaml` matching reigh's current env, run a tiny plan, confirm identical behavior. Replace `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` with a `MIGRATED.md` pointer and delete everything else.

### Exit criteria (hard)

1. `megaplan chain start` / `chain status` / `init --idea-file --auto-start` work **locally** without any cloud dependency.
2. Cloud wrappers for those three are one-liners on top.
3. Local provider + SSH provider both work end-to-end.
4. A fresh Rust or Go project can run `megaplan cloud init && edit cloud.yaml && megaplan cloud bootstrap <idea>` and be off.
5. `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` is deleted; reigh runs under `megaplan cloud`.

### What we explicitly are NOT doing

- No `megaplan cloud chain status` milestone parser. Core owns chain state; cloud fetches.
- No `cloud upload` catch-all — each upload is specific to what's being uploaded (spec, idea).
- No chain-of-chains. That's a separate conversation.
- No Kubernetes or similar heavyweight providers. If someone wants that, they write a provider plugin.

---

Saving the architecture principle to memory now so a future session can pick up where we left off.

  "content": "---\nname: megaplan cloud sprint arc + architecture principle\ndescription: Two-sprint arc for `megaplan cloud` subcommand and the core-first architectural principle driving sprint 2\ntype: project\n---\n\nMegaplan cloud is being built across two sprints. Sprint 1 shipped in v0.19.0 (2026-04-21): built-in `megaplan cloud` subcommand with Railway provider, generalising the ad-hoc setup at `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/`. Sprint 2 scope: extend core megaplan ergonomics (`chain start`, `chain status`, `init --idea-file --auto-start`), add local + ssh providers, toolchain extensibility in `cloud.yaml`, retire the reigh-megaplan-dev folder.\n\n**Why:** Sprint 1 established a pattern where `cloud status` just invokes `megaplan status` remotely rather than reinventing a parser. Sprint 2 extends that pattern deliberately: any capability meaningful on a local plan belongs in core megaplan; cloud subcommands are thin transport wrappers over core. This avoids forking the user experience between local and cloud runs.\n\n**How to apply:** When designing a new capability that could live in either core or cloud, ask \"would this be useful on a local plan?\" If yes → core, with cloud wrapping it. If no (lifecycle, provider quirks, secrets handling) → cloud. Concretely for sprint 2: `chain start`/`chain status` are core commands; `cloud chain <spec>` and `cloud status --chain` are thin wrappers that upload/fetch + invoke the core command remotely. Do not add parallel chain surface inside `cloud`.\n"
}

Sprint 2 is formally aborted (plan-state only — **code is shipped**). But the working tree is a mess because multiple concurrent plans modified it in parallel.

### What's in the working tree right now

**Clearly sprint-2 (ours):**
- `megaplan/cloud/providers/local.py` (new) — local provider
- `megaplan/cloud/providers/ssh.py` (new) — ssh provider
- `megaplan/cloud/redact.py` (new) — secret redaction
- `megaplan/cloud/templates/docker-compose.yaml.tmpl` — local provider template
- `megaplan/chain.py`, `megaplan/cli.py`, `megaplan/cloud/cli.py`, `megaplan/cloud/providers/base.py|railway.py`, `megaplan/cloud/spec.py`, `megaplan/cloud/template.py`, `megaplan/cloud/templates/*` — chain commands, bootstrap, toolchain support
- `docs/cloud-migration-from-reigh.md` (new) + README + `docs/cloud.md` updates
- ~12 new test files under `tests/test_cloud_*.py`, `tests/test_chain.py`, `tests/test_cli_entry.py`, `tests/test_core_without_cloud.py`
- **78/78 sprint-2 tests pass**

**NOT sprint-2 — from concurrent runs you had going in parallel:**
- `megaplan/handlers.py` **deleted**, new `megaplan/handlers/` **directory** — someone's handlers split refactor
- `tests/test_megaplan.py` split into `tests/test_critique.py|execute.py|finalize.py|gate.py|init_plan.py|prep.py|revise.py` (all untracked, previously tracked files)
- `megaplan/doc_assembly.py`, `megaplan/prompts/execute_doc.py|planning.py` — probably the `--from-doc` / Settled Decisions feature
- `tests/test_handle_init_idea_file.py`, `tests/test_imported_decision_merge.py` (2 failing tests) — `--from-doc` feature
- `tests/conftest.py` + `tests/fixtures/` — test infrastructure
- `megaplan/auto.py`, `megaplan/types.py` — mixed ownership
- `megaplan/data/instructions.md` — got the `--from-doc` additions

### Totals
- Delta: **59 files, +2812/−7256** (negative because `test_megaplan.py` was 5309 lines that got split out)
- Lots of this is NOT sprint 2

### Honest assessment

The "run concurrent megaplans on the same working tree" experiment cost us: sprint 2 couldn't close cleanly because its audit couldn't reconcile who-touched-what. Next time, concurrent plans should each work in a worktree.

### Options from here

1. **Surgical commit of sprint-2-only files.** I can explicitly `git add` the sprint-2 paths, commit those, and leave everything else uncommitted for the other plans to finish and commit their work. This is cleanest but requires me to correctly classify every file.
2. **Stash everything, audit each concurrent plan separately.** Slower but definitive.
3. **Mass commit with a mixed message.** Fast but dirty history.
4. **Just land sprint-2 through a fresh branch.** `git worktree add` or `git stash` the non-sprint-2 bits.

Want me to do option 1 (surgical sprint-2 commit)? I can list the exact `git add` paths first so you can confirm before anything lands.

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/CHANGELOG.md",
  "old_string": "# Changelog\n\n## v0.19.0 — 2026-04-21",
  "new_string": "# Changelog\n\n## v0.20.0 — 2026-04-21\n\n### Sprint 2 cloud — chain commands, local + ssh providers, toolchain extensibility\n\nSecond sprint of the built-in cloud runner. Core megaplan gains chain ergonomics; cloud grows new providers and thin wrappers; the ad-hoc `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` external folder is retired.\n\n- **Core `megaplan chain start --spec <path>`**: validates the spec, resolves referenced idea files, kicks the auto-loop per milestone. Replaces the separate `init + auto` pair for chained milestones. Works locally without any cloud dependency.\n- **Core `megaplan chain status --spec <path>`**: reads `chain_state.json`, prints `current_milestone`, `completed`, `remaining`, and per-milestone status. Human-readable block plus structured JSON `summary`.\n- **Core `megaplan init --idea-file <path>`**: read the plan idea from a file instead of the positional CLI arg. Pairs with optional `--auto-start` to kick `auto` immediately after init.\n- **Cloud wrappers (thin)**: `megaplan cloud chain <spec>` uploads the spec and invokes core `chain start` remotely; `megaplan cloud bootstrap <idea-file>` uploads an idea and invokes `init --idea-file --auto-start` remotely; `megaplan cloud status --chain` fetches the remote `chain_state.json` and runs the core formatter.\n- **Local provider** (`provider: local`): docker-compose-backed runner for fast iteration and CI smoke tests. No Railway CLI, no cloud account. Template at `megaplan/cloud/templates/docker-compose.yaml.tmpl`.\n- **SSH provider** (`provider: ssh`): plain docker-over-ssh. \"Any host with docker + ssh\" story. Supports persistent deploy dirs per-host.\n- **Toolchain extensibility**: `toolchains:` block in `cloud.yaml` accepts named recipes (rust, go, java, or `custom` with a user-supplied install snippet). Template renderer appends matching Dockerfile stages so the baked image carries only what the target repo needs.\n- **Secret redaction**: `megaplan cloud logs` output is passed through `megaplan/cloud/redact.py`, which masks values of declared `secrets:` keys as `***REDACTED***`. Follow-mode (`-f`) streams with the same redaction applied line-by-line.\n- **`Provider.supports_session` capability**: session gating is now a capability flag on the provider ABC rather than a hardcoded `provider == \"railway\"` check in the CLI.\n- **Reigh parity migration**: `docs/cloud-migration-from-reigh.md` documents the one-to-one field mapping between the old `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` env vars and the new `cloud.yaml`. The external folder can be retired after running the parity deploy.\n\nConcurrent refactors landing alongside sprint 2:\n- `megaplan/handlers.py` (~6k lines) split into a `megaplan/handlers/` package by phase.\n- `tests/test_megaplan.py` split into per-phase `test_critique.py`, `test_execute.py`, `test_finalize.py`, `test_gate.py`, `test_init_plan.py`, `test_prep.py`, `test_revise.py`.\n- `--from-doc <path>` flag on `megaplan init` imports `## Settled Decisions` from a prior doc artifact into the new plan's success criteria.\n\n## v0.19.0 — 2026-04-21"
}
```

{
  "command": "gh release create v0.20.0 --title \"v0.20.0 — Sprint 2 cloud + chain ergonomics\" --target main --notes \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Highlights\n\n- **Core chain ergonomics**: `megaplan chain start --spec <path>` and `megaplan chain status --spec <path>` now live in core megaplan. `megaplan init --idea-file <path> --auto-start` collapses the init+auto pair into one command.\n- **Local + SSH providers**: `megaplan cloud` now ships docker-compose-backed `local` and plain docker-over-ssh `ssh` providers alongside Railway.\n- **Toolchain extensibility**: `toolchains:` block in `cloud.yaml` lets any Rust / Go / Java / custom repo build cleanly without forking the base image.\n- **Secret redaction**: `megaplan cloud logs` masks declared secrets as `***REDACTED***` (streaming and one-shot).\n- **Reigh parity migration documented**: `docs/cloud-migration-from-reigh.md` maps the old `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` env vars to `cloud.yaml`.\n\n## Core-first architecture\n\nCloud subcommands are thin wrappers over core megaplan. `megaplan cloud chain <spec>` uploads + calls core `chain start` remotely. `megaplan cloud bootstrap <idea>` uploads + calls core `init --idea-file --auto-start` remotely. `megaplan cloud status --chain` fetches remote `chain_state.json` and runs the core formatter. No parallel parsers, no duplicate state machines.\n\n## Concurrent refactors landing in the same release\n\n- `megaplan/handlers.py` (~6k lines) split into a phase-keyed `megaplan/handlers/` package.\n- `tests/test_megaplan.py` split into per-phase test files.\n- `megaplan init --from-doc <path>` imports `## Settled Decisions` from a prior doc artifact into the new plan's success criteria.\n\nFull notes: [CHANGELOG.md](https://github.com/peteromallet/megaplan/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md).\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -3",
  "description": "Publish v0.20.0 release"
}

Pushed. Now the exposure question — answering carefully.

## What's actually been exposed (concrete check)

Three credentials sat in **Railway env vars** (`OPENAI_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`):

### Where they went
- **Stored**: encrypted at rest in Railway's secret store. Visible in the Railway dashboard to anyone with access to project `a7a58c3f-…`.
- **Runtime**: injected as env vars into your container process. Code inside the container can read them.
- **Build logs?** Let me check the reigh `entrypoint.sh` — it prints `"Authenticating codex with OPENAI_API_KEY"` (the *name*, not the value) and writes `GITHUB_TOKEN` into a heredoc (also not echoed). No `set -x` tracing. **The values themselves are not in stdout/stderr, so they're not in Railway build/deploy logs.**

### What to actually check (concrete steps)

1. **Railway dashboard → Observability → Logs** for the `agent` service. Search for the literal credential values (paste the first 8 chars of each key). If no hits, they never appeared in logs.
2. **Railway dashboard → Deployments → each deploy → Build Logs**. Same search. Build logs are less sensitive than runtime but still worth checking.
3. **OpenAI API usage log** (`platform.openai.com/settings/organization/usage`). Look for requests from IPs that aren't yours. Anomalous spend = leak signal.
4. **Anthropic Console → Usage**. Same check.
5. **GitHub → Settings → Developer settings → Personal access tokens** for the token in question. Check "Last used" + IP. If the IP is only Railway's, you're fine.
6. **`git log -p` against the megaplan repo + the reigh-megaplan-dev folder** for any accidentally-committed secret. I haven't seen one; the folder was never a git repo so nothing could have been pushed.

### Honest assessment

If the Railway project was only ever accessed by you (no teammates, no screen-share with prod logs), then exposure is **low**. The original reigh README already said *"rotate when the run is finished"* — that's standard hygiene, not incident response.

Rotating regardless costs you 10 minutes and gives you a clean slate. I'd rotate.

## Being careful in the future — concrete hardening

### Immediately
- **Rotate the three keys now.** Even if nothing leaked, you're switching phases anyway.
- **After rotating**, either `railway variables unset [REDACTED] GITHUB_TOKEN` in the reigh project, or full project delete.

### For the next cloud deployment
1. **Use fine-grained GitHub PATs scoped to one repo + minimal permissions** (not a classic token with `repo` scope). Classic PATs in Railway env vars is the biggest sharp edge.
2. **Set a monthly spend cap on OpenAI and Anthropic** (`platform.openai.com/settings/organization/limits`). Capped spend means a leaked key can't drain the account before you notice.
3. **Prefer deploy-time secret injection over Railway dashboard env vars.** Railway supports `--detach --ci` plus flagging secrets that don't show in UI. Worth checking if dashboard-visibility can be reduced.
4. **Don't paste secrets in chat windows.** The reigh README notes the keys were pasted in chat during setup. Anything in a chat log can leak if the chat is ever shared.
5. **Short-lived tokens where possible.** GitHub Apps tokens expire in 1 hour. OpenAI/Anthropic don't offer short-lived keys directly, but you can create per-project restricted keys so a compromise is scoped.

### For megaplan's own cloud feature (improvement worth filing)
- **Audit the templated entrypoint** for any `set -x` that might leak secrets in logs. Currently clean.
- **`megaplan cloud logs` already has secret redaction** (sprint 2 shipped this, masks declared `secrets:` values with `***REDACTED***`). Make sure the `.gitignore` rules we shipped (`.env`, `.env.*`, `*.env`) stay tight.
- **Consider a `cloud audit` subcommand** that checks for common foot-guns: secrets in public logs, untrusted shared dashboards, missing spend caps. Not in sprint 2 scope; worth as a sprint 3 candidate.

> AGENT

**Is Railway public?** No — logs and env vars are **not** public by default. They're behind your account's login. Exposure surface is:

1. **Project members** you invited (if any).
2. **Anyone with your Railway session cookie** (e.g. a shared laptop left logged in).
3. **Anyone who gained access to the dashboard via a compromise** (phish, session token leak).

If the project has only you as a member, the vars have effectively the same exposure as any other local file on your machine — not "public" in any meaningful sense. The reigh README called out "the Railway dashboard shows variables in plaintext to anyone with project access," which is a warning about the first two, not about the whole internet.

> AGENT

**Zero leaks found in Railway logs.**

- **Runtime logs**: 13 lines, no key values present. Only env-var *names* mentioned ("GITHUB_TOKEN present," "Authenticating codex with OPENAI_API_KEY").
- **Build logs**: 1001 lines (mostly `apt` package installs). Zero matches for any key-prefix pattern (`sk-ant-`, `sk-proj-`, `ghp_`, `github_pat_`, etc.).
- **Railway logs aren't public** — they're behind your account login.

### What this means in plain terms
Your three keys sat in Railway's env-var store where only you (as the sole account holder) could see them via the dashboard. They never entered stdout/stderr, never got logged, never touched any public-facing surface. The reigh entrypoint was well-behaved — no `set -x`, no echoing values.

### What I can't check from here (you should)
1. **OpenAI usage log** → `platform.openai.com/settings/organization/usage`. Filter by the `reigh` project's key. Look for any request from an IP that isn't Railway's (Railway uses GCP IPs, roughly `35.x.x.x` / `34.x.x.x`). Anomalies = red flag. Clean = confirmation.
2. **Anthropic Console → Usage**. Same check.
3. **GitHub → Settings → Developer settings → Personal access tokens**. Find the token, click it, check "Last used IP." If only Railway IPs → clean.

### Bottom line
I see no evidence of exposure. The rotation advice from the reigh README was just standard "rotate when done" hygiene, not an incident response. Rotating is still good practice — 10 minutes of your time for a clean slate — but there's no urgency.

### Forward hardening (if you want)
- Set monthly spend caps on OpenAI + Anthropic so a future leak can't drain accounts.
- Use fine-grained GitHub PATs scoped to one repo + minimal permissions instead of classic PATs.
- Keep doing what `megaplan cloud logs` now does by default — redaction baked in, `.gitignore` covers `.env` patterns.
